**Revision (2026-10-03, cycle-2 red team round 1) [assumed: review record]:** findings applied, each with its dated text in place and the earlier text kept or quoted. (1) **landing-02 with cross-section-01 (disclosure, needs the user):** single-proof mode admits one one-leaf landing per DEGRADE_AFTER, each at most 240 s of chain (MAX_VIEWS = 4 segments of at most one term each), so the backlog is replaceable from A1, depends on voluntary holders past RETAIN_SECONDS (L25) and is void at the landing horizon. No rule changes; C2-R12 gains the throughput bound, C2-R10 a sentence, §12's DEGRADE_AFTER row and §15's degradation row a qualification, and §16's R1 row the proposed limitation L-SPT, **[signed: D79 (1)]** (2026-10-04; earlier "[open: user sign-off], not signed"). The bound was first written as MAX_BLOCKS = 256 s (240 s with holder views only) on the premise that a non-final sentinel segment may span terms; that premise contradicted C7-R06, C7-R02 and S1-R16 (a sentinel block carries the term of its own timestamp, so each term is its own segment) and was withdrawn the same day, giving 240 s for every one-leaf landing [proven: C2-R12's corrected paragraph]; every copy (C2-R10, §12, §15, §16, S4-R03, S4-R08, S4-R14, S4-R15 E10, README) now reads 240 s and one thirtieth. (2) **slashing-01:** §15's reorg row no longer calls a false L1 reference class B; a reference reorged out or never canonical is no offence (S3d). (3) **slashing-05:** C2-R09 states that a replacement or dead-mode landing writes `hatchTip` (C3-R06 step 7), closing an unattestable honest RESUME after a same-block ordinary landing reset C2-R08's deferral, if that case is reachable (**[open]**, C3-R07; relabelled on the same day's recheck); C2-R08's formula is unchanged. (4) **landing-01:** the guest's exactly-these-request-blocks assertion becomes the derived output `fiBlocksMatch` (C2-R04's journal, a new rule paragraph, the guest paragraph, the partition table, the uniqueness claim and a contestability claim; C2-R13, C2-R14, §14 E12 (vi), §16's C3 row), so a false consumption landed by one unsound leaf in single-proof mode has a C2-R14 counterproof; for a hatch the value is true by construction. Where historical text says the guest asserts the kind-FI list, it is read with this entry. (5) **forced-inclusion-02:** C2-R02 states why `mixHash` and `difficulty` are not in `headerCore` (C7-R10, C7 §11 item 27). (6) **migration-bridge-01:** C2-R16's pin payment gains a floor at `firstEtnaTerm`, with a proven no-preactivation-debit paragraph and an [open] adjacent CONFLICT-restart case. (7) **cross-section-02 (disclosure, needs the user):** C2-R05 L4 states, as an upper bound, that one landing carries at most the first MAX_VIEWS terms of a sentinel object; §16's S1 row gains the open L4 sentinel boundary rule; LS1-2 is S1's proposed limitation, **[signed: D79 (2)]** (2026-10-04; earlier "[open: user sign-off]"). (8) **cross-section-03:** C2-R05 L8 places C3-R07's hatch checks at L1, L7 and inside L8; C3-R07's "cheapest first" is withdrawn; the W15 table gains the [open] item on L4 for view 254. B's obligations from these findings are in §16's table. No check order of an ordinary landing, no error and no slashing rule is added by this round except as stated.

**Revision (2026-10-04, S3 §15 item 18(g): the lock generation) [assumed: review record; open: B/J review]:** C7-R06 gains Rule G: a TIMEOUT, TERM_END or FALLBACK view change's lock lies in the generation its `closes` names (G1: the lock block's PH names `closes` at `(VC.t, VC.v)`; G2: the lock is the closed generation `X`'s own lock pair, `X`'s base, the honest case when the signers' highest certified block on `X`'s chain is `X`'s base, an empty `X` included, with `X` held, opening `(VC.t, VC.v)` and itself satisfying Rule G by a walk back along the run of closings sharing that lock pair to a G1 hop or a REPLACE, RESUME or `genesisOpening` base). C2-R05 L4 now states the `closes` linkage of `viewChanges[]` explicitly (the first `closes` equals `lastVCHash`, each later one the preceding VC's logical id, else `ViewChangeMismatch`, a reading of "the VC chain from `lastVCHash`" made explicit) and that every consumed TIMEOUT, TERM_END or FALLBACK satisfies Rule G, asserted by the guest at the first block under it (C2-R04's V2 assertion) and, for a closing with no block after it in the range, only through S2-R15's projection once adopted (W15 L4c row). The `recordViewChange` admission check for H and F records is a proposal to B (S2-R13) with C8's error binding (W15 row). No error, ABI or storage field is added by C2. Round 1 of review (2026-10-04): G2 walks the run (a lock-pair equality alone laundered a REPLACE-sibling lock through one empty view), and the W15 row's G2 admission supplies the run's preimages; the `closes` linkage is tagged as conditional on S2-R15's `lastVCHash` writer (W15 row 'C2-R05/R06'); the W15 row also records the proposal that L4c compare `(VC.t, VC.v)` with the pair `X` opens. Round 2 of review (2026-10-04): C7-R06 makes `genesisOpening` a walk base at C4's bootstrap lock pair (S2-V18's flow passes) and records two honest closings Rule G refuses (a lock on a late certificate of an earlier generation, a later committee's FALLBACK below `X`'s base), both already unlandable under L4's lock-equals-last-block rule **[assumed: reading of merged L4, C7-R06]**; the L4 'Lock generation' sentence now cites that rule for the lock-pair part and makes the trailing-VC claim conditional on L4c and C7 §11 item 28 (b)(7); C2-R04's guest list names the run's preimages as private witnesses. Round 3 of review (2026-10-04): C7-R06 records the honest closings Rule G refuses as a lower bound, adding a closing of a REPLACE or RESUME reopening locked above its base on the replaced generation, also unlandable under L4 **[assumed: reading of merged L4, C7-R06]**; C4-R12's CONFLICT-restart opening is an open base cell (C7 §11 item 28 (a)); Rule G's provisional home is S2-R10's check (ix) (reviewer J), after whose adoption L4 cites it; the L4 'Lock generation' sentence names the landing path, with `lastLanded` as the previous segment's last block at a landing boundary marked assumed as in C7-R06; the W15 row's G2 bases name the genesis hop's pair and bootstrap lock pair. Round 4 of review (B's review at `e8004cc`, 2026-10-04): (B-G-03) the W15 row's G2 admission needs each recursive edge's closed object with its full S2-R10 (i) to (viii) witness verified, or a named authenticated record with exactly equivalent checks and a defined writer; the earlier "one keccak per hop" is withdrawn and the per-uncached-edge cost is **[open: unmeasured]**; (B-G-02) the walk is bounded at V_MAX + 1 closing objects per term, not V_MAX hops; B's disposition (S2-R10 (ix) agreed as the single home, Rule G not adopted in S2 at that head, the formation step and the record interface prerequisites, B-G-04 blocking S3's (iii-a)) is recorded in C7 §11 item 28 (b)(0). Verifier round (2026-10-04) [assumed: review record]: the guest list and the W15 row cite Rule G's activation boundary (C7-R06; C7 §11 item 28 (b)(8)): Rule G binds only closings with `VC.t ≥ ACT_TERM`, a pre-`ACT_TERM` hop is a legacy base of the walk, and every lock vote verifies under the domain S3-R08's selector names.

**Revision (2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 2, CS-2, CS-3, L1-01, L1-03, L1-04) [assumed: review record; open: B/J review]:** findings applied, each with its dated text in place and the earlier text quoted. (1) **CS-2:** C2-R05 L4's per-term cap read "at most V_MAX per term"; a term whose views 0 to V_MAX all close carries V_MAX TIMEOUTs and one TERM_END or FALLBACK, V_MAX + 1 = 5 view changes, and with empty views the chain cannot be split (L1 needs a block, S2-R15 rejects trailing closings), so the honest landing reverted. L4 now counts closings by the closed view's term `VC.t`, at most V_MAX + 1, plus one per REPLACE or RESUME reopening of that term, names the revert, and gains vector E20; C6-MP's MP-05 bullets are corrected to match **[proven: from S2-R07 and S2-R11 as cited; the reopening bound stays open]**. (2) **CS-3:** C2-R05 L5 required "a certificate of committee(t)" at a FALLBACK lock; when the closed view is empty the lock is an earlier term's block that committee(t) never certified. L5 now takes the certificate of the committee of the term of the segment the lock ends (the previous segment's `termId`, which may precede the FALLBACK's `t`), and states that every FALLBACK in `viewChanges[]` is checked, including one closing an empty view; S2-R14 (ii) is B's, so its matching wording is a proposal in §16's S2 row. (3) **L1-01:** the checkpoint-or-record write depended on the accepting leaves but was ordered before L11. It now follows L11's `verify` (a view call, so the reorder adds no external state change) and is decided from the returned `zkLeaves_`: provisional iff single-proof mode and exactly one distinct ZK leaf accepted, `leafId` that leaf; two accepting leaves write the checkpoint. C2-R05 L10/L11, C2-R06's failure paragraph, C2-R12, C2-R13 and C2-R15's transaction-order sentence change to match; E16 gains (v). The proposed alternative (keep the order and decide from presented proofs) is the defect itself, so it is not taken. (4) **L1-03:** `LandInput.hatchAnchorNumber` is removed: a hatch's anchor A is the journal tip, and C3-R07 check 5 reads `l1AnchorTipNumber`, `l1AnchorTipHash` and `l1AnchorTipTimestamp` (T_A) as L7 verifies them. (5) **L1-04:** §11's CONFLICT edge cited "C3-R09 steps 3 and 4"; it now cites C3-R06 steps 1 and 3 (C2-R14 (c)); step 4 (`fiBlocksMatch`) is a derived output and may differ.

**Revision (2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 2, L1-01-R1) [assumed: review record; open: B/J review]:** L1-01's fix decided the landing path from L11's returned `zkLeaves_`, but `confirmProvisional` still read "a valid proof from another leaf" with no `kRequired` and no rule for judging the leaf, so a bundle {record leaf valid, garbage tagged as the other leaf} could be read as CONFIRMED (skipping DEGRADED_FINALITY, defeating L-PF) or as a false `ProofConflict` halting the chain. C2-R13 and C2-R14 now state that `confirmProvisional` calls `verify(keccak256(abi.encode(journal)), proof, 1)` and requires the returned `zkLeaves_` to name a ZK leaf other than `record.leafId`, presented tags counting for nothing, with the same **[open: C8 §3.1 question 33]** dependency; §10's comment matches, and E12 gains (viii). E16 (v)'s "leaf 1 can still raise a conflict" now rests on this stated rule, not on an assumption.

**Revision (2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 2, SL-2) [assumed: review record; open: B/J review]:** C2-R08 gains the `replaceableFrom` checkpoint ring `rfLog` (C8 slots 282 and 283), which S3d reads for A1 at a signed L1 reference, since later landings overwrite `replaceableFrom`'s inputs and no stored record kept them (S3-R08). One extra write per L1 block that changes an input, a ring overwrite after its first lap (C5-R06's exemption); gas **unmeasured**.

**Revision (2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 3, L1-R3-01, L1-R3-02) [assumed: review record; open: B/J review]:** (1) **L1-R3-01:** CONFLICT gets a stored input. Record `k`'s status becomes CONFLICTED (C8-R06 value 4) and the cursor word gains `conflictK` (C8-R15 slot 278), the lowest conflicted index. A conflict raised in CONFLICT on an earlier PROVISIONAL record `k′ < conflictK` is admitted and lowers `conflictK`; a conflict at or above `conflictK` and every confirmation revert `NotActive`. In CONFLICT the finalization pass stops strictly before `conflictK`, so a CONFLICTED record never finalizes, and the restart's `r` is the record at `first`, at or below `conflictK`. C2-R13, C2-R14, the rollback bound, §10, §11 and §14 E22 carry it. Earlier text: "no checkpoint write except by `finalizeProvisional` of records preceding the conflicting one", with only a bool CONFLICT flag stored and no rule for a second conflict. (2) **L1-R3-02:** both paths of `confirmProvisional` now require `first ≤ k < next` and status PROVISIONAL, else `NotConflict`. So CONFIRMED and FINAL records can be neither contested nor re-confirmed, a stale ring slot (`k ≥ next`) is never read, and the restart's `r` always exists. Before this, the precondition sat only in §11's state machine and C8-E12's pre-state. The cursor fields `first` and `next` are defined in C2-R13. Vectors: E22 (§14); C8-E12 and C8-E30 gain triggers (C8-traceability).

The order is kept and the text corrected. `NotActive` now applies, on a PROVISIONAL record `first ≤ k′ < next`, to the confirm path and to a conflict at `k′ > conflictK`; a call on a CONFLICTED record, either path, reverts `NotConflict` from check (1). Either way the call reverts and writes nothing; only the normative error name changes. Earlier C2-R14 text: "The confirm path, and any conflict at `k′ ≥ conflictK`, reverts `NotActive`", and the refused-call list's "`confirmProvisional`'s confirm path or a conflict at or above `conflictK`". The first revision line above (L1-R3-01, L1-R3-02), "a conflict at or above `conflictK` and every confirmation revert `NotActive`", is superseded on this point. ## 1. Scope, vocabulary and claim convention

**[assumed: section boundary]** C2 owns the one L1 landing action and what it carries; the proof-system set and its rotation; the canonical blob encoding and the journal; the inbox checks and the guest statement; the landing-based arbitration; the L1 checkpoint write with the provisional-finality rule and CONFLICT; the landing rewards as formulas (the seam to S3); the retention duty; and the origin authentication C1 consumes. C1 owns the L2 header profile, oracle, pins and reveals; C3 the forced-inclusion queue, its windows and landing gate, consumption, settlement and the hatch (C2 names only what a landing reads and writes there); C4 the migration and the shared-contract change table; C5 the frame-transaction gate; C7 the block-validity predicate V1 to V10, which the guest asserts and C2 cites by id; C8 indexes §10; S1 seats, sortition and the committee walk; S2 certificates, view changes and the FALLBACK, TIMEOUT and TERM_END gates; S3 the reserve sizing, slashing and every reward derivation; C6 the threat model, the limitations register and the unmeasured-numbers register. Their text is not repeated here (`00-decomposition.md` §1; D9, D10).

**[assumed: notation]** **Assumed** marks a proposed rule or premise; **proven** an argument made on this page, not a machine check; **open** an unresolved point with its closure condition. `C2-Rnn` has one normative definition. §12 is C2's only register of numbers. "Round n, Rn…" cites candidate A's red-team iterations; "after the #22188 review" cites A's revision on B's review of PR #22184 (2026-10-01).

| Term | Definition [assumed: vocabulary] |
|---|---|
| Landing | One L1 transaction, `land` or `landFor`, carrying a range of certified L2 blocks as blobs plus one proof, accepted by the existing Inbox proxy. |
| Range, segment | The blocks `[lastLanded.height + 1, endHeight]` a landing carries, chosen by the lander; a segment is the part in one view, `(termId, view, sequencer, firstHeight, lastHeight, fiCount)`. |
| lastLanded | The inbox's record of the newest landed block (§10, `LastLanded`). |
| Lander, recipient | Any address that sends a landing; no role, bond or registration. The recipient is the `rewardTo` the proof's journal binds (C2-R02, C2-R04), paid whoever the lander is. |
| Leaf | One ZK proof system among the verifier's ZK_N; a TEE leaf never counts. |
| Journal, record, anchor tip | The proof's public input (§3); one block's canonical bytes in the payload (§2); the newest L1 block any block of the range anchors, checked by the inbox against L1 history. |
| Provisional record, checkpoint | The stored record of a one-leaf landing (§6); the existing L1 SignalService tuple `(uint48 blockNumber, bytes32 blockHash, bytes32 stateRoot)`, whose L2 side and write semantics (C1-R06, C1-R07) C1 defines. |

**[assumed: named premises]** T1: L1 includes and orders transactions; total L1 censorship is outside the design (L12, L13). T2 (arguments page): L1 retains blob data for about 18 days (l1-dependencies page: the duration is preserved under shorter slots by EIP-8198, EIP-7782 is silent), and nodes derive within that window. A-ARCHIVE: bytes of landed ranges older than the T2 window are held by archive operators (archive and RPC nodes, anyone who derived them in the window); nothing enforces or rewards it, and a node syncing after the window needs such a holder (as C1's A-ARCHIVE). T3 (arguments page): ZK soundness of the leaves that accept a journal. A-L1HIST: `blockhash` within 256 blocks and the EIP-2935 contract within 8,191 return canonical L1 hashes at landing time; keccak is collision resistant (an L1 fact the pages fold into L7; named here, not a page premise id). T11: fewer than Q colluders in any committee (S2); C2 relies on it only in single-proof mode and for "certified implies executed by Q attesters" (V6). C3 cites T11 only, for C3-R06's landability claim (a VALID_GOSSIP chain, which a certified chain is under T11, is landable up to the gate), and no longer cites A-CERT; C3's bound, origin progress and at-most-once execution use no committee premise (C3 §1, §9; D41). A3: the ZK leaves are independent implementations; a TEE attests nothing about soundness; a common-mode guest bug is outside A3 (L7). A-CERT: a certificate passing L5 is Q(m) signatures of the committee S1 pins for the term over the end header hash (S2). A-FI: C3 defines the queue, the windows and the landing gate (C3-R04), the consumption L8 checks and the settlement and replayable bits it writes (C3-R03, C3-R06), and the hatch (C3-R07), at `76c6150` under D41; due-ness, the stall clock and the void set of the earlier head are withdrawn by D39. A-EXEC: builder, importer and guest implement the same C7 predicate and C1 profile.

## 2. The landing action and the canonical encoding

### C2-R01. One action, lander-chosen ranges, no seal

**[assumed: proposed rule]** `land(LandInput)` (anyone; `rewardTo` in calldata) or `landFor(rewardTo, LandInput)` (only the frame gate G_LAND, C5) carries a range of certified blocks as blobs attached in range order plus one proof, and is the only L1 action that advances `lastLanded`. There is no propose step and no sequencer seal: the batch commitment is the digest every party computes from the certified chain (C2-R02), and the committee's view change pins where a view ends (S2). Anyone may land any certified prefix spanning at most MAX_VIEWS consecutive views, MAX_BLOCKS blocks and MAX_BLOBS blobs; several landings may sit adjacent in one L1 block; an oversized landing is not includable, never slashable. Deadlines are economic only: nothing voids by clock inside the horizon (C2-R10) and no rule penalizes a holder for not landing (invariant I5, S3). Checks run cheapest first and the proof last, so a race loser without the gate pays C5-R15's D-FLOOR(L) plus its own blob fees (C2-R20, D72 7a) **[assumed: arbiter answer, D72]** and the proof's revert rolls back every write. (Landing §1, §3, §8; round 4, D9; the no-seal design from the judge synthesis of the B4 round.) **Adversary schedule:** a withheld seal (none exists); range splitting for reward (C2-R16); a stale copy after the parent moved (`NotNext`, free under the primary shape, C2-R20).

### C2-R02. Canonical blob encoding

**[assumed: proposed rule]**

```text
payload = 0x03 || u32(numBlocks) || Σ_i ( u32(len(rec_i)) || rec_i )
rec_i   = headerCore_i || seqSig_i (65 B) || [viewChangeBytes_i if first block of a view] || txListBytes_i
FI rec  = headerCore_i || seqSig_i || [viewChangeBytes_i] || u48(i)  (no txListBytes; i the entry id, C3-R05(b))
```

`headerCore` is the non-derivable subset of the signed header PH and the block header: timestamp, coinbase, gasLimit, anchorNumber, anchor hash (the header's `parentBeaconBlockRoot`), carried certificate height and hash, termId, view, kind, vcHash, txListHash. Every block carries `vcHash` here and only here, so the guest rebuilds PH from this record and the block header. The header's `mixHash` (`prevRandao`) is not in `headerCore` because it is derivable: C7-R10 fixes it as `keccak256(abi.encode(uint256 parent.difficulty, uint256 number))` for every block, and the guest recomputes it from the parent header [assumed: inherited Shasta/Unzen derivation; revision note 2026-10-03, finding forced-inclusion-02]. `difficulty` is likewise omitted as an execution output (the block's zk gas used under Unzen), which rests on C7 §11 item 27 [assumed]. `txListBytes` are the exact compressed bytes whose keccak is `txListHash` (the sequencer's own zlib stream, never re-compressed). A kind-FI request block's record has no `txListBytes` and ends in the `u48` id `i` of the entry it executes: its `PH.txListHash` is `keccak256(abi.encode(DOMAIN_FI, uint48 i, dataHash_i))`, `dataHash_i` derived as C3 §1 states it (for a LEGACY id from the retained `BlobSlice` and the committed codec revision), and its transactions derive from the entry's payload witness, which hashes to `dataHash_i` (C3-R05(a), (b)), so the record is about 250 bytes plus the 6-byte trailer and no request block adds a blob. Request records, replay blocks' included, count toward V10 (C3 §10, D41). Packing is today's blob coder, 130,044 usable bytes per blob, zero-padded, split in order. `||` here is byte concatenation of fixed-width fields; every protocol hash elsewhere is keccak256 over `abi.encode` of typed fields (interfaces page, convention adopted from PR #22188). (Landing §2; FI record without payload, round 6 fix pass; `kind` added, L1 state root and seat dropped, issue #22147.)

**[proven: encoding determinism up to the witnesses, conditional on V1, on S2-R10's logical id and on C3's payload witness]** No byte of the payload is the lander's choice except, where it holds more than one, which valid witness it carries: the sequencer's signature witness of a PH, and the quorum witness bytes of each view change it carries (S2-R10's witness, D16 and D21). Framing is fixed; each record's fields are signed by the sequencer (PH), committed by `txListHash`, or, for `viewChangeBytes`, fixed in their logical fields by the opening's logical id `vcHash`, which PH names (S2-R10 as D21 amends it: the ten logical words, `redraw` and `signerTerm` among the witness, not the id); `seqSig_i` and the view change's witness are the ones the lander received. The certified identity of a block is `phHash` (S2-R03), not the signature over it, and the identity of an opening is its logical id, not the quorum that signed it: a sequencer can produce two valid low-s ECDSA signatures of one PH (two nonces), and C7's V1 recovers the leader from either without choosing one witness; likewise one opening may travel with two valid quorum witnesses (another bitmap or aggregate, another redraw count, or for a FALLBACK another signer term), and D21 puts every one of them outside every hash, so PH, `phHash` and the journal's `vcHashOfFirstBlock` do not change with it. So two landers of one range produce blobs identical in content and in every byte except those witnesses, "identical up to the signature witness and the quorum witness bytes of the view changes it carries", and the versioned hashes are a function of the certified block set, the range and the witnesses carried. Both land the same state: the journal's end state is the same, `blobHashesRoot` differs, the guest accepts either (C2-R04), two signatures of one PH are not equivocation (S2's equivocation evidence is two different PHs for one height, S2-R03), and two witnesses of one opening prove one opening object, never two (S2-R10, D21). A misencoded landing yields an end hash different from the certificate and fails L5, self-harm only. (Weakened from "bit-identical" after B's W8 verdict on #22196 (2026-10-01), B-C2-02; the quorum witness added after B's re-verdict on #22196 (2026-10-01, comment 5934018060), non-blocking item, under D23.)

**[assumed: proof reuse, stated once here; C2-R04 binds it]** A proof binds its recipient: `rewardTo` is a journal field (C2-R04), so a proof is per recipient, not per lander. Anyone may submit the same proof with that fixed recipient: the submitter pays the L1 gas and the blob fee, the bound recipient receives the reward (C2-R16), and a different recipient needs a new proof. A proof also binds the blob bytes it was made over (the journal commits `blobHashesRoot`), so a reused proof travels with the blobs it was made over, witness included. Throughout this section "reuse" of a proof means submission with its fixed recipient and its fixed blobs. (B's W8 verdict on #22196 (2026-10-01), B-C2-01: "a prover makes a proof for Alice, Bob copies it and substitutes Bob's recipient: either verification fails, or the recipient binding is absent"; verification fails, round 2, S6.)

**[assumed: limitation, the codec fixes no unique signature witness]** The alternative B named is to commit the exact envelope and signature bytes into the certified identity, so that one PH has one landable record. It was not taken because the attested object would then depend on a value the sequencer alone chooses after attestation, which reopens the identity question S2-R03 closed by making `phHash` the identity. Residual: two proofs may be needed for one range if two witnesses circulate, a signature witness of a PH or a quorum witness of a view change the range carries (D21 keeps the latter outside every hash, as committing it would make the id depend on which aggregate a party happened to hold), since each proof binds the `blobHashesRoot` of the bytes it was made over; the lander uses the witness it received, and the first landing included wins (C2-R09). C6 carries this on the accepted-limitations register (§16); vector E09 (§14) fixes the case. (B's W8 verdict on #22196 (2026-10-01), B-C2-02.)

**[assumed: bounds consumed]** C7's V9 bounds every record to 130,035 bytes, so a framed single block (nine framing bytes) fits one blob and any certified block is landable alone at every slot time; further records add 4 framing bytes each. C7's V10 bounds a term's sequencer-block records to TERM_BYTES_MAX = 5 × LANDING_UNIT; kind-FI request records, replays included, count toward it (D41); hatch blocks (view 254) are exempt from V10, the guest skipping it for view 254 (C3-R07), and bounded by HATCH_K per hatch. MAX_ENVELOPE on the P2P layer equals V9 (S2). (Round 2, H1; round 3, H3; round 4, R4H-6; round 6, R6H-3.)

## 3. Proof systems and the journal

### C2-R03. The proof-system set, image sets and rotation

**[assumed: proposed rule]** The verifier holds ZK_N leaves (RISC0 and SP1 at launch, a third the target) and accepts a landing in normal mode only when ZK_K = 2 distinct ZK leaves accept the same `journalHash`; a TEE leaf never counts in any mode. Each leaf keeps today's staticcall shape plus `require(remote.code.length > 0)` and an image set `{current, previous, previousValidUntil}`; a DAO rotation moves the old set to `previous` with `previousValidUntil = now + ROTATION_OVERLAP`, or zero when flagged emergency. `verify(journalHash, proof, kRequired)` reverts unless `kRequired` distinct ZK leaves accept; `kRequired` is ZK_K except in single-proof mode (C2-R12). **[assumed: arbiter answer, D72]** The revert is `BadProof` (§10), raised whenever fewer than `kRequired` distinct presented leaves accept, which covers a TEE leaf where a ZK leaf is required, a proof under an image past its validity, and `kRequired = 0` or `kRequired > ZK_N`. A proof under the previous image counts while `block.timestamp <= previousValidUntil` (the boundary is inclusive). Only presented leaves are called; a presented leaf whose remote verifier has no code (its `require(remote.code.length > 0)` failing) counts as rejecting and does not by itself make `verify` revert. On success `verify` returns in `zkLeaves_` the ids of the presented leaves that accepted; the encoding of that id set in the `uint8` return is not fixed by D72 **[open: C2/C8, C8-R10]**. `imageSets(_leaf)` for an id that is not a ZK leaf (the TEE leaf, or an id at or above ZK_N) reverts `UnknownLeaf`. (Landing §7; TH1, TH20.) **Adversary schedule:** one forged proof (needs a second independent leaf, A3); a retired image trusted for ROTATION_OVERLAP after a non-emergency rotation (accepted, landing §14). Vector: E13 (§14).

### C2-R04. The journal and what the guest proves

**[assumed: proposed rule]**

```text
Journal { chainId, protocolParamsHash,
          parentBlockHash, parentStateRoot, parentPhHash, grandparentPhHash, firstHeight,
          endHeight, endBlockHash, endStateRoot, endPhHash, endParentPhHash,
          blobHashesRoot, segmentsRoot, vcHashOfFirstBlock,
          l1AnchorTipNumber, l1AnchorTipHash, l1AnchorTipTimestamp,
          fiHeadBefore, fiHeadAfter, fiVoidCount,   (C3-owned, C3-R06; D41)
          fiConsumedHash, fiReplayHash,             (C3-owned, C3-R06 step 3; D41)
          fiBlocksMatch,                            (derived output, bool; C3-R06 step 4, C3-R08; landing-01, 2026-10-03)
          [fiParentTimestamp]          (forced journal only, view 254, the hatch; C3-owned)
          rewardTo }
journalHash = keccak256(abi.encode(Journal));  leaf input = hashPublicInputs(journalHash, leaf, address(0), chainId)
```

`fiParentTimestamp` (`uint48`) is C3's field (C3-R07 check 5 at `76c6150`): the timestamp of `lastLanded`'s block, which `LastLanded` does not store, so the lander supplies it in the forced `LandInput` (§10); the guest asserts it against `lastLanded`'s header (below). It sits after `fiBlocksMatch` (after `fiReplayHash` before the landing-01 revision of 2026-10-03) and before `rewardTo`, and only in the forced journal, the hatch's, which is domain-separated from the ordinary journal and binds `rewardTo` (C3-R07); an ordinary journal has no such field and the two encodings never collide.

`protocolParamsHash = keccak256(abi.encode(params))` over every guest-visible constant published by `protocolParams()` on the inbox (TH25; Anchor page §8): a mismatched guest yields no valid proof, a liveness failure, never a soundness one. `parentBlockHash`, `parentStateRoot`, `parentPhHash` and `grandparentPhHash` are `lastLanded`'s (`blockHash`, `stateRoot`, `phHash`, `parentPhHash`), from storage; `parentPhHash` and `grandparentPhHash` are what the guest's V3 clause (i) compares a carried certificate against at the start of a range (below; D23). `endParentPhHash` is the `phHash` of block `endHeight − 1`. Its calldata source is the typed field `LandInput.endParentPhHash` (§10): the lander, who rebuilt the range's PHs to prove it, supplies it; the inbox writes that word into the journal it hashes, and C2-R06 stores the same word as `lastLanded.parentPhHash`, so the inbox stores exactly what it hashed. The guest computes it from block `endHeight − 1`'s rebuilt PH when the range holds two or more blocks, and proves it equal to the journal's `parentPhHash` for a one-block range; a wrong word has no valid proof, so the next landing's `grandparentPhHash` is a value a proof established, never a lander's witness. (B's W12 verdict on #22196 (2026-10-02, comment 5945458948), item 2: at `6e2b732` the field had no calldata source, and for a multi-block range neither the blob versioned hashes nor the end certificate reveal it.) The forced-inclusion fields are C3's (C3-R06 at `76c6150`, D41): `fiHeadBefore` (storage, the queue head), `fiHeadAfter` and `fiVoidCount` (`LandInput`, checked by C3-R06 steps 1 and 2), `fiConsumedHash` (the inbox, over the stored entries `[fiHeadBefore, fiHeadAfter)`) and `fiReplayHash` (the inbox, over the stored entries named by `LandInput.fiReplayIds`), each hashing `(i, kind_i, dataHash_i, s_i)` with `(kind_i, dataHash_i)` as C3 §1 derives them, the legacy derivation included (C3-R06 step 3). The run, clear-tip, stall and due fields of C3 at `4aa0299` are withdrawn (D39, D41). `blobHashesRoot = keccak256(hashes)` over the blob versioned hashes the inbox reads with `blobhash(i)`; `segmentsRoot = keccak256(segments[])` as L4 resolves them; `vcHashOfFirstBlock` is 0 when the range continues an open view; `rewardTo` is bound for every landing kind, so a proof is per recipient as C2-R02 states it: a copied proof with a swapped reward address fails verification (round 2, S6), and anyone may submit the proof with its bound recipient (B-C2-01). The inbox reads no mutable storage into either forced-inclusion hash: entries, their kinds, data hashes and save times never change once saved (C3-R01), so both hashes are fixed by the ids. `deadline` is calldata only.

**[assumed: proposed rule, landing-01, 2026-10-03]** `fiBlocksMatch` (`bool`) is a derived output. For an ordinary range (every landing kind but the hatch), the guest first asserts, as for any journal, that the witnessed blobs' recomputed versioned hashes match `blobHashesRoot`; this assertion holds whatever value `fiBlocksMatch` takes, so the payload the guest reads is fixed by a fixed input. It then decodes the payload's records and lists the range's kind-FI blocks in chain order from each record's `kind` and its `u48` id trailer (C2-R02). This needs no queue preimage and no execution. Before it compares anything, and so before it can output either value, it asserts that its private witness `replayMeta`, the claimed replay list's complete metadata `(i, kind_i, dataHash_i, s_i)` per id in order, satisfies `keccak256(abi.encode(chainId, replayMeta)) == fiReplayHash`, C3-R06 step 3's encoding, `fiReplayHash` being a fixed input; with no witness that satisfies it there is no proof of either value. It then compares that list with the claimed list: the ids of `replayMeta`, which the assertion binds, then `[fiHeadBefore + fiVoidCount, fiHeadAfter)`, which are fixed inputs and need no preimage (`fiConsumedHash`'s preimage is read only on the true branch, as before). (Revision 2026-10-03, B-W22-A01: this sentence read "the ids of `fiReplayIds` (the preimage of `fiReplayHash`)"; that preimage is `(chainId, (i, kind_i, dataHash_i, s_i)…)`, not the ids alone, the fixed inputs carry only its hash, and a guest that returned `false` before binding the list could be given another list under the record's hash. **[proven: from the assertion, conditional on keccak collision resistance (A-KECCAK, C6; corrected 2026-10-03 on B's recheck of `e6b795c`, earlier "(A-L1HIST's keccak)", A-L1HIST being the canonical-history assumption)]** For a given `fiReplayHash` a sound guest proves a journal, of either value, only on the one replay list the hash commits, so a substituted list yields no `fiBlocksMatch = false` journal and no C2-R14 conflict.) If the two are equal, it sets `fiBlocksMatch = true` and proves the rest of the journal as the next paragraph states. If they differ, or the bound payload does not decode into records, it sets `fiBlocksMatch = false`, executes nothing, and outputs zero in every other derived output. The fixed inputs are copied as in any journal. For a hatch (view 254) the guest decodes no payload: the hatch has no blobs, and its blocks are one per id of `[fiHeadBefore + fiVoidCount, fiHeadAfter)` by C3-R07's derivation, with `fiReplayIds` empty (C3-R06 step 1), so the guest sets `fiBlocksMatch = true` by construction. A forced journal is never provisional (C2-R11), so the hatch never meets C2-R14. The inbox writes the constant `true` into the journal it hashes for every landing kind. It reads no calldata for this field and adds no error. A proof of a `false` journal does not verify against the hashed `journalHash`, and the landing reverts `BadProof` at L11 (C2-R03). A `false` journal therefore never lands, and its one use is as a C2-R14 counterproof.

The guest proves, over blobs whose recomputed versioned hashes match `blobHashesRoot`: decoding yields blocks `firstHeight..endHeight`; `segments[]` (bound by `segmentsRoot`) is the split of the range into maximal runs of blocks with equal `(termId, view)`, each with its recovered sequencer, first and last heights and FI count; each rebuilt PH's `seqSig` recovers to the view's leader (V1 as C7-R05 states it: any valid signature of the PH, the guest fixing no witness, C2-R02) and each header's `extraData` carries that PH's `(termId, view)`, the block's kind and the fee-sharing byte (C1-R03, H1); each `parentBeaconBlockRoot` is non-zero and equals the keccak of the witnessed L1 header numbered `PH.anchorNumber` (H2, V5); the first block of a view carries `vcHashOfFirstBlock`; every block executes VALID from the parent state to exactly its `blockHash` (V6); hashes chain from `parentBlockHash` to `endBlockHash`, whose header carries `endStateRoot`; every anchored L1 header lies on a parent-hash chain ending at the journal's tip, which is the anchored header with the greatest `anchorNumber` in the range, its number, hash and timestamp the three tip fields (a chain of at most MAX_L1_HEADERS_PER_LANDING headers, intermediate non-anchored headers counted; a lander splits a range whose anchor span exceeds the cap, so liveness is unaffected), anchor numbers monotone from the parent's, V5 as C7-R09 applies it (at every block against its own timestamp; D14's inheritance clause is superseded by D41 and withdrawn in C7-R09 at `0f0a2f0`, so no block inherits a verdict); timestamps obey V4; for view 254 (a hatch) the guest skips exactly V1 (no sequencer signature), V2 (no term window), V4's upper bound (a hatch block's timestamp is `ts_0 + j`, C3-R07) and V10 (C7-R13; C2-R02's bounds note), as C3-R07 states them, and asserts the rest of the list below, C3-R07's derivation included (kind FORCED, `termId = termOf(ts_0)`, one entry per block per C3-R05(a) and (d)); V5 holds at every hatch block at its own timestamp, no block of any kind inheriting a V5 verdict (C3-R07's claim and C7-R09 at `0f0a2f0`; DRIFT_MAX = 0, C2-R19 and §12); the guest additionally proves `fiParentTimestamp` equals the timestamp of `lastLanded`'s header (C3-R07 check 5). For an ordinary range with `fiBlocksMatch = true`, the kind-FI blocks are, in chain order, the replay blocks for the ids of `fiReplayHash`'s preimage, then one block per id of `[fiHeadBefore + fiVoidCount, fiHeadAfter)`, and the guest asserts each per C3-R05 (a), (b)'s record and `txListHash`, (d) and (e) (C3-R08); that the list is exactly this one, with no other kind-FI block, is the output `fiBlocksMatch` of the paragraph above, not an assertion (landing-01, 2026-10-03; the earlier text asserted it with "and no other kind-FI block appears"); for every kind-FI block, executed or replay, the guest asserts `s_i ≤ T_{A_b}`, `s_i` from the hash preimage and `T_{A_b}` the anchor timestamp it already authenticates for V5 (C3-R08). Contiguity against `head(A_b)` stays gossip validity, which the guest does not assert (C3-R08). Sequence, rights, certificate, blob hashes, anchor tip, queue state and deadlines stay outside the proof (C2-R05); execution, derivation, the anchored L1 headers, FI contents and per-block signatures are inside it.

**[assumed: exactly which rules the guest asserts, D17]** Of C7's predicate the guest asserts, per block: H1, H2 and H3 (C7-R02 to C7-R04); V1 (C7-R05, the recovery only, no witness fixed), V2 (C7-R06, the window, the `vcHash` chain, the `(termId, view)` chain rule as D22 item 2 restores it, Rule G at the first block under a TIMEOUT, TERM_END or FALLBACK opening whose `VC.t ≥ ACT_TERM` (C7-R06's activation boundary, C7 §11 item 28 (b)(8) **[assumed: proposed rule; open: B, C8]**: a pre-`ACT_TERM` closing is not judged by Rule G, and a pre-`ACT_TERM` hop is a legacy base of the walk) (added 2026-10-04; G2 reads each closed object of its run with its full witness, verified under S2-R10 (i) to (viii) in the guest, at most V_MAX + 1 closing objects per term (C7 §11 item 28 (d), open; revised 2026-10-04 on B-G-02/B-G-03 from "the logical preimages of its run, one per hop, at most V_MAX per term"), supplied by the lander as private witnesses beside `viewChangeBytes`, as in the W15 row; the guest cost per uncached object is **[open: unmeasured]**), and the opening-object field only; the guest recomputes `vcHash` from the logical fields of `viewChangeBytes`, the quorum witness outside it (S2-R10 as D21 amends it); the opening's committee verification over that witness is L4's check on L1), V4 (C7-R08), V5 as C7-R09 applies it, V6 (C7-R10), V8 as C3-R05(b) and C3-R08 state it at `76c6150` (C7-R11), V9 (C7-R12) and V10 (C7-R13), with the view-254 skips above; of V3 (C7-R07) only the field, by the block's carried object (D23): (i) a block that carries a certificate `C`: `carriedCertHeight ∈ {n − 1, n − 2}`, keccak of `C`'s preimage (S2's certificate encoding, which the lander supplies to the guest as a private witness, outside the blob) equals `PH.carriedCertHash`, and `C`'s inner field `C.phHash` equals the `phHash` of the ancestor at `carriedCertHeight` on the chain being proven; (ii) the first two blocks under an opening (C7-R06; this includes the same-view REPLACE and RESUME reopenings, and the block after GENESIS_CHILD falls under (i), as C7-R04's GENESIS_CHILD row says and the genesis exception below decides): `PH.carriedCertHash` equals the block's own `PH.vcHash`, the opening's logical id (S2-R10 as D21 amends it); (iii) kind FORCED, sentinel-255 and GENESIS_CHILD blocks: no carried object exists (C7-R04's mode matrix) and the clause is skipped. Which of (i) and (ii) applies is decided from bound inputs, not from the range's shape. A block whose `PH.vcHash` is the genesis child's value (C7-R04's GENESIS_CHILD row; C7 item 14, a protocol parameter bound by `protocolParamsHash` once C4 and S2 fix it), which by C7-R06's later-block rule is every block of view 0 of the first Etna term `t_0` from the GENESIS_CHILD's child up to the first opening, applies (i) with no opening witness: that value names no opening object, so no ten-field preimage exists for it. Every other block's `PH.vcHash` names an opening object (C7-R06), and only then do the witness and the test apply: the lander supplies the opening's ten logical fields as a private witness that must hash to the block's `PH.vcHash`, and the guest applies (ii) if and only if `n − lockHeight ∈ {1, 2}` for that opening's lock, otherwise (i); so a block at any other height that carries the id in place of a certificate has no valid proof, and a range that starts at the second block under an opening (`vcHashOfFirstBlock = 0`, the first block being `lastLanded`) is still provable. The ancestors clause (i) reads are on the proven chain inside the range, except for the first two blocks of a range: the ancestor at `firstHeight − 1` is `lastLanded`, whose `phHash` is the journal's `parentPhHash`, and the ancestor at `firstHeight − 2` is `lastLanded`'s parent, whose `phHash` is the journal's `grandparentPhHash` (both from storage, L3; the latter stored by the landing that ended at `lastLanded` from its proven `endParentPhHash`, C2-R06); where the guest reads either ancestor's PH fields, the lander supplies that PH as a private witness that must hash to the journal field, never one matched by block hash alone. A match by block hash would not bind the ancestor: one execution hash can sit under two envelopes with different `vcHash` or `carriedCertHash`, hence two `phHash` values (S2-R03), so a certificate of a sibling envelope of `lastLanded`'s parent, certified by a quorum outside T11, would pass at the start of a range and fail inside a range that holds that parent, and VALID_LANDED would depend on where the lander splits ranges. So `C(lastLanded)` carried by block `firstHeight` or `firstHeight + 1`, and `C(firstHeight − 2)` carried by block `firstHeight`, are decided against bound values, and a block gets the same verdict in every range that carries it. At the first Etna range `lastLanded` is C4's genesis parent; the range starts with the GENESIS_CHILD, which skips the clause, and its child's ancestor at `n − 1` lies in the range. C4's initial `lastLanded.phHash` and `lastLanded.parentPhHash` are bound storage like any other, so the verdict does not depend on the split there either, and no honest block compares against them: the genesis parent and its parent are pre-Etna blocks that no Etna certificate names, so no block carries a certificate of either, whether the GENESIS_CHILD lands alone (the next range's `grandparentPhHash` then names the genesis parent) or not. The earlier wording, "the carried hash equal to the ancestor's `phHash`", compared a certificate's hash with a header's hash, two different preimages, so no honest block satisfied it and a check that did hold would have been on the wrong value; B's re-verdict on #22196 (2026-10-01, comment 5934018060) found it, and D23 names the inner field. The guest verifies no carried certificate: no popcount, no aggregate signature, no committee membership of any intermediate certificate; the end certificate is the inbox's check (L5, C2-R05), verified on L1. V7 is this rule's binding. So what a landed range satisfies is VALID_LANDED = C2-R04 plus C2-R05 by rule id, and not VALID_GOSSIP, C7's full predicate before landing: an importer accepts a landed range by the L1 record, read at the L1 reference C7-R07 fixes for an accepted parent and never at the node's own L1 head (D22 item 4), even where a carried intermediate certificate would fail the verification half of C7's V3. A failing carried certificate in a landed block is objective evidence against its holder whose certificate preimage the accuser supplies from gossip and which must hash to the carried hash in `headerCore` (C7-R15; S3's obligation, D17 and D22 item 6); the closure is conditional on publication, and a landed block whose carried certificate preimage was never published is C7's residual (C7-R15), unslashable outside T11 (C7's A-QUORUM). Under clause (i) above the prover held that preimage, since the guest hashed it, but nothing on the landing path makes the prover publish it, so the residual stands as C7 states it. (Landing §4; round 6, R6H-1; issue #22147; the V5 application and the view-254 skips after J's review of #22199 (2026-10-01), J-1 and J-3, under D14; the exact list and the two predicates after B's W8 verdict on #22199, D17.)

**[assumed: the journal's partition, stated once here; C2-R13, C2-R14, §11 and vector E12 cite it]** Every journal field is in exactly one of two classes. A **fixed input** is a value the inbox takes at landing from its own storage or constants, from L1, or from calldata that the guest never computes (checked by it, as `fiParentTimestamp`, or only bound, as `rewardTo`). A **derived output** is a value the guest computes from the fixed inputs. The source column names where the inbox takes the value it writes into `abi.encode(Journal)`; `journalHash` is the hash of exactly that encoding, and the verifier sees no field except through it (§10). `deadline` is calldata, not a journal field.

| Journal field | Class | Source of the value the inbox hashes |
|---|---|---|
| `chainId`, `protocolParamsHash` | fixed input | inbox constants (`protocolParams()`) |
| `parentBlockHash`, `parentStateRoot`, `parentPhHash`, `grandparentPhHash` | fixed input | storage, `lastLanded` (L3) |
| `firstHeight` | fixed input | storage, `lastLanded.height + 1` (L1) |
| `blobHashesRoot` | fixed input | L1, `blobhash(i)` (L6) |
| `fiHeadBefore` | fixed input | storage, C3's queue head (C3-R06) |
| `fiHeadAfter`, `fiVoidCount` | fixed input | `LandInput`, checked by C3-R06 steps 1 and 2; the guest compares them, with `fiReplayIds`, against the range's request blocks and reports the result as the derived output `fiBlocksMatch` (C3-R06's claim as revised for landing-01, 2026-10-03; earlier: "and by the guest (C3-R06's claim: lander inputs that the guest and the inbox check)") |
| `fiConsumedHash` | fixed input | the inbox, from the stored entries `[fiHeadBefore, fiHeadAfter)` with `(kind_i, dataHash_i)` per C3 §1 (C3-R06 step 3) |
| `fiReplayHash` | fixed input | the inbox, over `LandInput.fiReplayIds` and their stored entries (C3-R06 step 3); `fiReplayIds` is unique for a range, the range's request-block ids below `fiHeadBefore` (C3-R06's claim) |
| `fiParentTimestamp` (forced journal only) | fixed input | `LandInput`, checked by the guest against `lastLanded`'s header (C3-R07 check 5) |
| `rewardTo` | fixed input | `LandInput.rewardTo` for `land`, `_rewardTo` for `landFor` |
| `endHeight` | derived output | `LandInput` (the payload's block count; checked by L1 and L5) |
| `endBlockHash`, `endStateRoot`, `endPhHash`, `endParentPhHash` | derived output | `LandInput` (`endPhHash` checked by L5) |
| `segmentsRoot` | derived output | the inbox, `keccak256(segments[])` as L4 resolves them |
| `vcHashOfFirstBlock` | derived output | the inbox, from `viewChanges[]` as L4 resolves them; 0 when the range continues an open view |
| `l1AnchorTipNumber`, `l1AnchorTipHash`, `l1AnchorTipTimestamp` | derived output | `LandInput` (the hash checked by L7) |
| `fiBlocksMatch` | derived output | the inbox, the constant `true`; a `false` journal fails L11 (C3-R06 step 4; landing-01, 2026-10-03) |

The landing's checks on derived outputs (L1, L5, L7, L8) decide whether a landing is accepted; they do not move a field into the fixed class.

**[proven: on the ordinary and provisional path, every derived output is a function of the fixed inputs, conditional on the soundness of the leaf that accepts the journal and on A-L1HIST (keccak)]** Each private witness of the guest is bound by a hash to a fixed input, or decides acceptance only: the execution witnesses to `parentStateRoot`; the ancestor PHs to `parentPhHash` and `grandparentPhHash`; the opening's logical fields to a `PH.vcHash` in the blobs; the L1 header RLPs to the anchor hashes in the blobs, the tip being the newest of them; the FI payload witnesses to the `dataHash_i` values of the consumed-entry and replay preimages, which hash to `fiConsumedHash` and `fiReplayHash`, computed by the inbox from entries that never change once saved (C3-R06 step 3); a carried certificate's preimage decides acceptance only. Under D41 the guest takes no answer about the queue as input: `fiHeadAfter`, `fiVoidCount` and `fiReplayIds` are lander inputs that the inbox checks by range and gate, and every forced-inclusion field is a fixed input (C3-R06's claim). Whether they describe the range's request blocks is the derived output `fiBlocksMatch`, a function of the claimed list and the payload, which the guest asserts against `blobHashesRoot` in both branches (true by construction for a hatch); with `false` every other derived output is zero, so for any fixed inputs the sound guest admits exactly one value of `fiBlocksMatch` and, with `false`, exactly one journal (landing-01, 2026-10-03; the earlier sentence said "lander inputs that the guest and the inbox check", which left a false claim with no journal at all and so no conflict). So a sound guest admits at most one journal for given fixed inputs: two journals equal in every fixed input, each accepted by a sound leaf, are equal (C3-R06's claim, which C2-R04's partition and C2-R14 (c) cite). A forced journal is never provisional (C2-R11), so C2-R14's predicate never meets `fiParentTimestamp`. The uniqueness is scoped to that path, the one C2-R14's conflict compares: for a hatch the anchor the hatch party chooses is not among the listed fixed inputs, so distinct valid hatch anchors can produce distinct tips with the listed fixed fields unchanged. Since a forced journal is never provisional, that cannot raise a false CONFLICT (B's W8 on #22218, comment 5952257755, clarification). (B's W12 verdict on #22196 (2026-10-02, comment 5945458948), item 1, asked for this audit of the complete journal. The derived outputs beyond the three end fields of the earlier C2-R14 are `endHeight`, `endParentPhHash`, `segmentsRoot`, `vcHashOfFirstBlock` and the three tip fields; the after-landing queue fields of C3 at `4aa0299` were derived outputs too, and under D41 the queue fields are fixed inputs. A false value in any of them, with the same fixed inputs, is as much a contradiction as a false end state.)

**[proven: a false forced-inclusion claim has a counterproof, conditional on T3 for the counterproving leaf and on T2 for the blobs; landing-01, 2026-10-03]** Let a one-leaf record's `fiHeadAfter`, `fiVoidCount` or `fiReplayIds` not describe the range's request blocks: an id claimed executed or replayed with no block in the range, a block in the range for an id claimed void or not claimed, or a wrong order. The record's journal has `fiBlocksMatch = true`, the only value the inbox hashes. On the record's fixed inputs the sound guest decodes the same blobs, finds the lists unequal and outputs `J′` with `fiBlocksMatch = false`; `J′` equals the record in every fixed input and differs in a derived output, so it is a C2-R14 conflict and the record does not finalize. The counterprover needs only the blobs, public for about 18 days under T2 (longer than DEGRADED_FINALITY), the record's fixed inputs, and the replay list's metadata `(i, kind_i, dataHash_i, s_i)` for the record's `fiReplayIds`, which the guest binds to `fiReplayHash` before either branch (above) and which the inbox keeps for every id below the head, `kind_i` and `dataHash_i` derived for a legacy id and `s_i` fixed at save (C3 §1, C3-R01) **[assumed: the entry fields are immutable after save, as C3 states]**; it needs no certificate preimage, no queue payload and no execution witness, so C2-R14's T11 residual does not cover this case (revision 2026-10-03, B-W22-A01: the witness claim read "the blobs ... and the record's fixed inputs" and omitted the replay metadata). So in single-proof mode one unsound leaf cannot finalize a consumption, void or replay that the range's blocks do not bear out, unless no other sound leaf proves `J′` within DEGRADED_FINALITY, which is the mode's own premise (C2-R14, L-PF) and not a new one.

**[proven: binding, conditional on T3 (ZK soundness of the accepting leaves), A3 and A-L1HIST]** No state root is finalized without valid proofs over a journal binding the parent root, the parent's and grandparent's phHash (storage), the blob hashes (`blobhash`), the anchor tip (`blockhash` or EIP-2935, L7), the consumed and replayed entries (storage; L8, C3-R06 step 3), and the certified end hash (L5). No third party can make a proven landing revert through the queue, provided it lands within HATCH_DELAY of its proof and consumes every entry saved before the proof and every entry that expires by its landing: all opens and closes are fixed in advance by storage, and a landing, a hatch or a `fiClear` between proof and inclusion is a free revert of a stale proof, as any race is (C3-R06's claim, C3-R09).

## 4. What the inbox checks

### C2-R05. Inbox checks L1 to L11, in order

**[assumed: proposed rule]** (Landing §3.)

- **L1 sequence.** `firstHeight == lastLanded.height + 1`, else `NotNext`; at least one block (`endHeight >= firstHeight`) and at most MAX_BLOCKS blocks, more reverting `RangeTooLarge` **[assumed: arbiter answer, D72]**; no one-per-L1-block rule.
- **L2 deadline** (degraded shape only, C2-R20). `deadline == 0 || block.timestamp <= deadline`, else `DeadlineExceeded` **[assumed: arbiter answer, D72]**.
- **L3 parent link.** `parentBlockHash == lastLanded.blockHash`; the journal's parent hash, root, `parentPhHash` and `grandparentPhHash` come from storage (`lastLanded.blockHash`, `stateRoot`, `phHash`, `parentPhHash`), never calldata.
- **L4 segments, views and rights.** `segments[]` lists up to MAX_VIEWS consecutive segments (more reverts `RangeTooLarge`); `viewChanges[]` is the VC chain from `lastVCHash` through every boundary, each VC's lock equal to the previous segment's last block, every TIMEOUT VC has `VC.v < V_MAX` (S2-R07: no TIMEOUT at `v = V_MAX`; this applies to every view, so no TIMEOUT closes view 254 or 255) and every TIMEOUT, TERM_END or FALLBACK VC has `VC.v ≤ V_MAX` (no holder view above V_MAX exists) unless `VC.v ∈ {254, 255}`, else `ViewChangeMismatch` **[assumed: proposed rule and error reuse; added 2026-10-04, CS-2-R1]**; **sentinel and hatch views are exempt (2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 2 fixes) [assumed: proposed rule]:** a TERM_END or FALLBACK VC whose closed view is the sentinel view 255 or the hatch view 254 is not refused by `VC.v ≤ V_MAX` and does not count toward the V_MAX + 1 bound below, which counts holder views 0 to V_MAX only; at most one such closing per `(VC.t, VC.v)` follows from the `closes` linkage as for a holder view **[proven, conditional on the `closes` linkage below]**; for view 254 the exemption is vacuous at L4, a hatch carrying no view change (the view-254 disjunct below) (earlier "every TIMEOUT, TERM_END or FALLBACK VC has `VC.v ≤ V_MAX` (no view above V_MAX exists)" with no exemption, which read literally refused any closing of view 255); hence at most V_MAX + 1 TIMEOUT, TERM_END or FALLBACK VCs of holder views per term counted by the closed view's term `VC.t` (one closing for each of views 0 to V_MAX: V_MAX TIMEOUTs and one TERM_END or FALLBACK, S2-R11) **[proven, conditional on the `closes` linkage below: a REPLACE or RESUME closes the generation it reopens, so of the generations of one `(t, v)` only the last is closed by a TIMEOUT, TERM_END or FALLBACK, and each view number of a term carries at most one such closing whatever the reopenings]**, a further closing VC of one `VC.t` reverting `ViewChangeMismatch` **[assumed: proposed error reuse]** (corrected 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 2, CS-2, from "at most V_MAX per term", under which the honest chain `[TE(t−1), TO(t,0..3), TE(t,4)]` of a term with five empty views, five closings of `VC.t = t`, was unlandable; vector E20; revised 2026-10-04, CS-2-R1: the CS-2 allowance "plus one more for each REPLACE or RESUME reopening of a view of `VC.t` in the chain, the reopenings themselves bounded by S2 and not here [open: S2, a per-term bound on reopenings; C6-MP MP-05]" is removed as unneeded, since the linkage already makes V_MAX + 1 tight, and the per-VC view checks are added, without which a colluding committee(t) quorum's `TO(t, V_MAX)` opened the nonexistent view `(t, V_MAX + 1)`, E20 (iv); the per-landing count of REPLACE and RESUME VCs stays MP-05's open item), each equal by logical id `vcHash` (S2-R10 as D21 amends it) to the first live VC recorded on L1 for its `(t, v, closes)` if one exists, whatever witness either carries (a REPLACE or RESUME record is dead once `lastLanded.height > vc.lockHeight`, C2-R09), a VC unequal to that record reverting `ViewChangeMismatch`, each witness verified as S2-R10 states it, a quorum of the committee of its signer term at its redraw count, under the FALLBACK gate, precedence and certificate-record rules S2 owns (round 5, R5S-5; round 6, R6S-3); S2-R14's L1 gates on a FALLBACK are checked here at landing, not in validity, which verifies a FALLBACK from its own fields and witness (D22 item 4); a FALLBACK failing that gate reverts `FallbackBelowCertificate` **[assumed: arbiter answer, D72]**. **Lock generation (added 2026-10-04, S3 §15 item 18(g)) [assumed: proposed rule]:** the chain is linked by `closes` **[assumed: conditional on W15 row 'C2-R05/R06' (S2-R15's `lastVCHash` writer); the linkage reads `lastVCHash` as that writer leaves it]**: `viewChanges[0].closes == lastVCHash` and each later VC's `closes` equals the preceding VC's logical id, else `ViewChangeMismatch` (the existing "VC chain from `lastVCHash`", stated explicitly; a sentinel segment's `ETNA_DEAD` opening and S2-R15's bootstrap and hatch branches keep their own entry rules, W15 row 'C2-R05/R06'); and every consumed TIMEOUT, TERM_END or FALLBACK VC satisfies C7-R06's Rule G (provisionally; on B's adoption of S2-R10 (ix) this cites S2-R10 (ix), C7 §11 item 28 (b)(0); B agrees that home at `e8004cc` but has not adopted Rule G there; its lock in the generation `closes` names: G1, or G2 with its walk back along the run of closings sharing the lock pair). L1 storage cannot read the lock block's PH, so Rule G is not an L1 check here: the guest asserts it at the first block under each such VC as part of C7-R06's V2 (C2-R04), and a VC with no block after it in the range is held to it only partly. Its lock pair is already pinned by this L4's rule that each VC's lock equals the previous segment's last block: the last block of `X` on the landing path (in the range, or `lastLanded` when `X`'s blocks landed earlier), or, when `X` has no block on that path, `X`'s own lock pair, which is G2's lock-pair test **[proven: from this L4; assumed: `lastLanded` as the previous segment's last block at a landing boundary, the reading C7-R06 marks assumed]**. That the lock's PH names `X` by full id, separating a same-`(t, v)` REPLACE or RESUME reopening of `X`, needs S2-R15's L4c and opening-subrun projection **[proven, conditional on that adoption; open: owner adoption, W15 L4c row]**; that `(VC.t, VC.v)` is the pair `X` opens, G1's `(termId, view)` equality and G2's opened-pair test, needs also C7 §11 item 28 (b)(7)'s addition to L4c **[open: proposal to B]**, so until both are adopted a trailing VC is held to G1 without that equality. Without the projection, a same-`(t, v)` REPLACE or RESUME reopening is not a segment boundary, so a `[VC]` naming `closes = X` with its lock on the reopening's branch passes the linkage and the lock-equals-last-block test and is refused only by the guest's Rule G assertion at its successor's first block **[open: until L4c is adopted]**. Each segment's sequencer satisfies `isHolder(t, v, sequencer)` as S1 computes it from the domain version effective at `termStart(t)` (round 4, R4H-1), or, with sentinel view 255, the term is open-empty, the chain in dead mode and the sequencer bonded, or the term in no-committee mode and the sequencer its sortition holder, any bonded address after the economic deadline (round 2, S9), or, with view FORCED = 254, the landing is a hatch (C3-R07): the view-254 segment is the range's only segment, `viewChanges[]`, `endCert` and `blobHashes` are empty and its `sequencer` is the zero address (C3-R07's Content: coinbase 0, no `seqSig`), and its rights are not a holder's but C3-R07's check 1 (`windowOpen(T)`, or `T > replaceableFrom` and `T ≥ hatchAt + HATCH_RESUME_GRACE`, else `HatchNotPermitted`, with (4a)'s non-maximal wait) and check 2 (`rewardTo`'s balance against HATCH_BOND, else `HatchNotBonded`), which run inside L8 in C3-R07's evaluation order, not here; a view-254 segment in any other shape (beside another segment, under a view change or certificate, or with a non-zero `sequencer`) fails this disjunct, and `isHolder` is false at view 254, so it reverts `NotHolder` (added 2026-10-04, external review by smtmfft, #22191 comment 5406603154; earlier the clause had no view-254 branch, so read literally every hatch reverted `NotHolder`) **[assumed: proposed rule; proven: by inspection of C3-R07's shape list, L4 refuses no landing C3-R07 accepts]**; **a hatch segment reads no committee (added 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 2 fixes) [assumed: proposed rule]:** for a range passing the view-254 disjunct L4 reads no `committeeRoot[termOf(ts_0)]` pin, runs no walk, writes no pin and makes no ROLE_HORIZON test, so `TermBeyondHorizon` and an unpinned `termOf(ts_0)` never refuse or slow a hatch; the committee sentence below applies to every other segment only, and the term's pin is left to `recordAssignment` or a later non-hatch landing, as for any term with no landing (vector E21 (v)); a sequencer meeting none of these reverting `NotHolder`; sentinel segments open with `keccak256(abi.encode(bytes32("ETNA_DEAD"), lastLanded.blockHash, uint32 t))`. **[proven as an upper bound: from L1, L3 and this check; cross-section-02, 2026-10-03]** Because a sentinel segment opens only from the current `lastLanded` and a range's segments are its maximal `(termId, view)` runs, one term per run, one landing carries at most the first MAX_VIEWS terms of a sentinel object's chain, and once any landing moves `lastLanded` the rest of that chain is unlandable (S1-R16's landable prefix; the recovery rate is S1-R17's, LS1-2 proposed). **Corrected 2026-10-03 (B-W22-A05) [proven: from C2-R02 and C7-R06]:** "the first MAX_VIEWS terms" reads "at most MAX_VIEWS occupied terms of the object, not necessarily consecutive, and at most 240 blocks": a term with no block under the object has no segment, so no bound on elapsed time follows. A continuation rule that would let one object land over several landings is a research candidate, not adopted ([sentinel continuation](../research/sentinel-continuation.md), **[open: research]**). Whether two consecutive sentinel segments of one object need a view change at their boundary, and which `t` the opening check recomputes, is not yet stated here (§16, S1 row; **[open: A/C2, with C8]**); until it is, one landing may carry only the object's first term. Committees (for every segment but a hatch's, which reads none, above) are the inbox's own pinned walk `committeeRoot[t]`, pinned by `recordAssignment(t)` or by the first landing that settles `t` (an unpinned term inside the horizon is walked in the same transaction); the committee proof's key hashes are checked against that root, never taken from calldata. A term older than ROLE_HORIZON is read from its pin; never pinned, it is unlandable and reverts `TermBeyondHorizon`, a term `t` being older than ROLE_HORIZON when `block.timestamp > termStart(t) + ROLE_HORIZON` (C2-R10) (B-W18-A01's arbiter ruling: start-based, matching S1-R14's `recordAssignment` window and S1-R09's retention). **[assumed: arbiter answer, D72]** for the names `RangeTooLarge`, `ViewChangeMismatch`, `NotHolder`, `FallbackBelowCertificate` and `TermBeyondHorizon` and for the horizon's reference point. D72 places `NotHolder` at "L6's rights check"; L6 has no rights check, so this text reads it as L4's rights check, the only check of that kind C2-R05 states **[assumed: reading of D72's label for `NotHolder`]**. **Replacement classification (added 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 4, CS-R4-01, CS-R4-02) [assumed: proposed rule and error reuse]:** L4 derives `isReplace`, true iff (a) `viewChanges[]` holds a REPLACE VC whose lock `(lockHeight, lockPhHash)` equals `(lastLanded.height, lastLanded.phHash)`, a REPLACE opening from `lastLanded`, or (b) the range carries a view-255 segment that this check admits under its dead-mode disjunct (S1-R17, tested at the sentinel object's L1 reference, S1-R16), or (c) the range carries a view-255 segment that this check admits under S1-R18 (b)'s post-deadline disjunct, or under the open-empty (S1-R15) or no-committee holder-path (S1-R18 (a)) disjunct when S1-R16's certificate-mode scan finds a certificate-mode term `u` with `lastLandedTerm ≤ u ≤ termOf(block.timestamp)`, read as S1-R16 states (each term's mode from its pin, an unpinned term inside the horizon walked, a scan that would walk more than SETTLE_MAX unpinned terms counted as finding one, an unpinned term older than ROLE_HORIZON counted as not in certificate mode) **[assumed: proposed rule, D40(3); proven: by inspection, (b) and (c) together are exactly the view-255 landings S1-R15, S1-R16, S1-R17 and S1-R18 call replacements]**; a RESUME VC, a hatch (view 254, C3-R07) and an open-empty or holder-path segment whose scan finds no certificate-mode term do not make it true (earlier "a RESUME VC, a hatch (view 254, C3-R07) and an open-empty or no-committee view-255 segment do not make it true", which let a sentinel landing that voids a certified backlog still inside its landing window carry `replace = 0` and skip L9, the hole D40(3) closed; red-team cycle 2 round 4 second recheck, CS-R4-01 (D40(3)), 2026-10-04). A REPLACE VC in `viewChanges[]` whose lock is not `lastLanded`'s pair reverts `ViewChangeMismatch` **[assumed: proposed rule; an honest REPLACE locks on `lastLanded` at its reference (C2-R09's record rule, S2-R15) and between restarts `lastLanded` only advances, so no honest landing is expected to carry a REPLACE locked elsewhere; not proven here]**. This does not bound the REPLACEs per landing: an empty reopened view leaves the lock pair at `lastLanded` (the lock-equals-last-block rule above), so `[REPLACE₁(closes X), REPLACE₂(closes Xr)]`, both locked on `lastLanded`, passes every L4 test, and the per-landing count stays MP-05's open item **[proven: from this L4's linkage and lock rules]**. **The binding REPLACE (added 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 4 recheck of CS-R4-01) [assumed: proposed rule; open: S2 confirmation, S2-R15 step 1]:** when (a) holds, (a)'s REPLACE VC is the last REPLACE VC in `viewChanges[]` (every REPLACE there being locked on `lastLanded` by the rule above), the reopening whose view the range's blocks continue; earlier REPLACEs in the same landing are consumed but bind nothing (earlier "so a landing consumes at most one REPLACE", which did not follow; red-team cycle 2 round 4, CS-R4-01 recheck, 2026-10-04). `LandInput.replace` must be nonzero and equal to (a)'s REPLACE VC's `l1Ref` (nonzero by S2-R09) when (a) holds, else to the sentinel object's L1 reference `(l1RefNumber, l1RefHash)` (S1-R16's five-word object) when (b) or (c) holds (earlier "to (b)'s sentinel object's L1 reference ... when (b) holds"; red-team cycle 2 round 4 second recheck, CS-R4-01 (D40(3)), 2026-10-04), and zero in both words otherwise, a RESUME landing included; any other value reverts `ViewChangeMismatch`. That L4 recomputes the dead-mode or (c) sentinel object with this reference rather than the three-word form above is the open S1-row item of §16 **[open: A/C2 with C8]**. **[proven: from this check, for (a); (b) and (c) conditional on that open item, (c) also on S1-R16's scan as stated]** `replace` is a function of the view changes and segments L4 has verified, not a lander's choice: (a)'s `l1Ref` is a logical field of a VC whose logical id L4 verifies and the first block's PH names (S2-R10, C2-R02). L9 (the A1 gate), C2-R08's forfeiture, `Landed.replaced` (§10) and S2-R15 step 1's reader read `isReplace`, (a), (b) or (c), which on an accepted landing equals `replace` nonzero; C3-R06 step 7's `hatchL1`/`hatchTip` write reads the narrower "(a) or (b)", so a landing that is `isReplace` by (c) only does not establish the RESUME marker, as open-empty and no-committee landings never did (C2-R09; B's Low on `982aac6`), and a RESUME resuming from it is attestable through C3-R09's A1 disjunct only **[open: whether an earlier landing in the same L1 block can make that disjunct false, as for C3-R07's same-block claim]** (earlier "L9 (the A1 gate), C2-R08's forfeiture, `Landed.replaced` (§10), C3-R06 step 7's `hatchL1`/`hatchTip` write and S2-R15 step 1's reader read `isReplace`"; red-team cycle 2 round 4 second recheck, CS-R4-01 (D40(3)), 2026-10-04). A RESUME-only path, one satisfying none of (a), (b) and (c), carries `replace = 0` and is not a replacement; a chain that also consumes a REPLACE VC opening from `lastLanded` is governed by the iff rule above, and the binding REPLACE need not be the chain's final closing (B's W8 on #22253, Low 4; earlier "RESUME landings carry `replace = 0` and are not replacements"): L9 does not gate them, which keeps C2-R09's pre-A1 RESUME route after a window hatch. (Earlier no rule tied `replace` to `viewChanges[]`, and L9 "(only if `replace` is set)" was its only check: a landing consuming a REPLACE VC with `replace = 0` skipped A1 and every replacement effect, and an ordinary landing with `replace` nonzero after A1 forfeited an honest announcer's bond and wrote `hatchTip`; vectors E23 (i) to (iii); (c) is E23 (viii).)
- **L5 end certificate.** `endCert.height == endHeight`, `endCert.phHash == endPhHash`, `popcount(bitmap) >= Q(m)` for the last segment's committee of size `m` at the certificate's redraw count, aggregate signature valid (one pairing); any of these failing reverts `BadCertificate` **[assumed: arbiter answer, D72]**. Intermediate segments ended by a TIMEOUT, TERM_END, REPLACE or RESUME VC need no certificate; one ended by a FALLBACK VC needs a certificate of committee(t) at the FALLBACK lock, or the lock equals `lastLanded` (round 5, R5S-5). **Corrected 2026-10-04 (red-team cycle 2 round 2, CS-3) [assumed: proposed rule]:** "committee(t)" reads "the committee of the term of the segment the lock ends", the previous segment's `termId` (L4: the lock equals that segment's last block), at the certificate's redraw count, which is earlier than the FALLBACK's `t` when the closed view is empty (S2's dark-committee case: `F(t,0)` locked at `h`, the last block of term `t − 1`, certified only by committee(t − 1)); and every FALLBACK VC in `viewChanges[]` is checked, including one closing a view with no segment, unless its lock equals `lastLanded`. **[proven: from L4 and S2-R09 (1)]** the honest FALLBACK's lock certificate exists under this reading, and the combined landing `[TE(t−1)@h, F(t,0)@h, blocks of (t+1,0)]` needs no prior landing to `h`. S2-R14 (ii)'s "required certificate" is B's text, and the matching wording is B's CS-3 change, merged under D86 (#22245; §16, S2 row, closed) **[assumed: review record, D86]** (relabelled 2026-10-04, B's re-verdict on #22246 at `55b4911`; earlier "the matching wording is a proposal (§16, S2 row) [open: B adoption]"). Sentinel views and hatches (view 254): an empty certificate, and the verifier never degraded (C2-R11). Verified on L1, not in the guest, so the withholding defence never rests on ZK soundness.
- **L6 blobs.** `blobhash(i) == blobHashes[i]` for `i < n <= MAX_BLOBS` (more than MAX_BLOBS listed reverts `RangeTooLarge` **[assumed: arbiter answer, D72]**); `blobhash(n) == 0`; `blobHashesRoot = keccak256(hashes)`.
- **L7 anchor tip.** `l1AnchorTipNumber < block.number` and `>= lastLanded.anchorTipNumber`; the hash equals `blockhash` within 256 blocks, else the EIP-2935 contract within 8,191; older is refused, `AnchorTooOld` (TH17).
- **L8 forced inclusion.** `fiHeadBefore` equals the queue head; then C3-R06, which C3 alone defines (B's W12 re-verdict, comment 5945991491); the summary that follows is a reading aid, and C3-R06 governs wherever the two differ: the range checks of step 1 (`FiRange`: `fiHeadAfter ≤ tail`, at most MAX_FI_PER_LANDING consumed, `fiVoidCount` at most the consumed count, and `fiReplayIds` at most MAX_FI_PER_LANDING strictly increasing ids below the head, each with its replayable bit set, empty for a hatch); the walk and gate of step 2 (`FiVoidNotExpired`, `FiGateUnmet`), which applies to ordinary, sentinel, dead-mode, replacement and hatch landings alike; `fiConsumedHash` and `fiReplayHash` from storage (step 3); settlement per C3-R03 (step 5): the void range burned, each executed entry's bounty credited to the includer's pull ledger, replays settling nothing, each replay's and each re-consumed entry's replayable bit written; the head, `base` and `short` (step 6) and `hatchTip` (step 7). A hatch also passes C3-R07's checks, at the positions C3-R07's evaluation order gives: its check 3 is L1, the anchor-tip part of its check 5 is L7, and its checks 1, 2, 4 (with (4a)) and check 5's hatch bounds run here, after C3-R06 steps 1 and 2 and in that order; C2-R05's order is the only fixed order for a hatch; `NotActive` stays outside L1 to L11 **[assumed: proposed rule; revision of 2026-10-03, finding cross-section-03]**. (Round 6, R6H-1 at `4aa0299`; D41.)
- **L9 replacement** (only if `isReplace`, L4, its sentinel disjunct (c) included (D40(3), red-team cycle 2 round 4 second recheck, CS-R4-01 (D40(3)), 2026-10-04); revised 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 4, CS-R4-01, earlier "(only if `replace` is set)", a lander's choice). C2-R08: `block.timestamp > replaceableFrom`, else `NotReplaceable` **[assumed: arbiter answer, D72]**.
- **L10 effects.** C2-R06, except the L1 checkpoint or provisional record (C2-R15, C2-R13), which runs after L11 (revised 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 2, L1-01; earlier "C2-R06").
- **L11 proof.** `verifier.verify(journalHash, proof, kRequired)` per C2-R03 and C2-R12; its revert is `BadProof` (C2-R03) **[assumed: arbiter answer, D72]**. Then, from the `zkLeaves_` it returns, the inbox writes the L1 checkpoint (C2-R15) or, if `kRequired = 1` and `zkLeaves_` names exactly one distinct ZK leaf, stores the provisional record with `leafId` that leaf (C2-R13); presented leaves that did not accept count for nothing. `verify` is a view call, so running it before this write adds no external state change **[proven: from C2 §10's `view` declaration]**; the decision needs `zkLeaves_` to identify the accepting leaves, not only count them **[open: C8 §3.1 question 33's encoding]** (added 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 2, L1-01).

**Adversary schedule:** a landing that skips the current request once its window is open (L8's gate, `FiGateUnmet`, TH7); a void before expiry (`FiVoidNotExpired`); a replay of an id without its replayable bit or a second replay (`FiRange`, C3-T21, C3-T23); uncertified blocks (L5); a stale parent (L1, L3); a non-canonical tip (L7); a committee or holder from calldata (L4); a replacement before A1 (L9); a REPLACE-consuming landing labelled ordinary, or an ordinary landing labelled a replacement (L4's `replace` equality, `ViewChangeMismatch`; CS-R4-01, CS-R4-02); an open-empty, holder-path or post-deadline sentinel landing that voids a certified backlog labelled ordinary to skip A1 (L4's (c), `ViewChangeMismatch`, then L9; D40(3), E23 (viii)).

### C2-R06. Landing effects

**[assumed: proposed rule]** On acceptance: `lastLanded = {endHeight, endBlockHash, endStateRoot, endPhHash, parentPhHash: endParentPhHash, termId, view, at: block.timestamp, l1AnchorTipNumber, l1AnchorTipTimestamp}`, each value taken from the journal being the word the inbox hashed into it (C2-R04's source column): `parentPhHash` is `LandInput.endParentPhHash`, the journal's proven `endParentPhHash`, becoming the next landing's `grandparentPhHash`; the term record of every settled term (at most SETTLE_MAX); the forced-inclusion writes of C3-R06 steps 5 to 7 (the head, `base`, `short`, the status and replayable bits, the includers' pull ledger and `hatchTip`; L8) and, for a hatch, C3-R07's effects (`hatchAt`, `hatchL1`, and `lastLanded` moved with `lastLanded.at` and `lastLandedTerm` untouched); `lastLandedTerm` (read by S1's dead-mode rule); the assignment digest and committee root of every settled term not yet pinned; the L1 checkpoint or provisional record (C2-R15); lander and attester rewards (C2-R16); MISS once per `(t, v)` per TIMEOUT-kind VC consumed, at settlement (S3-R07, MISS_PENALTY in S3 §11); the `prevrandao` mix (S1); `Landed`. S2's lock reset triggers on a landed replacement or hatch only, never on a record (C3-R07; S2-R15 in [S2](S2-certificates-and-handoff.md), merged under D78: the lock is reset only by observing a landed replacement or forced batch, never by a record, S2-R06 and S2-R15's reset paragraph; revised 2026-10-03 at the S2/S3 adoption, earlier "S2-R15's wording pending S2"). (Landing §3 L10; round 4, R4H-5.)

**S2-R15 projection (D92; added 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 4, CS-R4-01) [assumed: proposed; open: B/J review]:** S2-R15's mature reset (steps 1 to 4) reads what this rule writes and nothing C2 adds for it: `lastLanded` (C8-R15 slots 258 to 262), `lastVCHash` (275), C3-R06 step 7's `hatchL1` in the `FiQueue` word (256) and `hatchTip[N]` (281), and the `Landed` event (C8-R07).
