A successful landing is a replacement recovery trigger iff C2-R05 L4's `isReplace` holds, which on an accepted landing equals `replace` nonzero and `Landed.replaced = true`; a dead-mode landing is `isReplace` (b), and a post-deadline landing, or an open-empty or holder-path landing whose S1-R16 scan finds a certificate-mode term, is `isReplace` (c) (D40(3); red-team cycle 2 round 4 second recheck, CS-R4-01 (D40(3)), 2026-10-04; earlier only (b) was named, so a backlog-voiding sentinel landing read as a non-trigger); a RESUME landing, an ordinary continuation and an open-empty or holder-path landing whose scan finds none are not triggers; a hatch is the view-254 path (C3-R07). S2-R15 step 1 as merged under D94 (#22252) consumes all three disjuncts (a), (b) and (c) of this `isReplace` from the authenticated replayed path, and never from the supplied `replace` or `Landed.replaced` alone ("neither a lander-supplied nonzero `replace` field nor an already consumed historical REPLACE proves a new recovery"); it selects the last consumed REPLACE for the (a) binding, as L4 does (E23 (vii)) **[assumed: S2 text at D94]** (earlier: "So S2-R15 step 1's 'a replacement has nonzero `LandInput.replace`' now reads a derived value, not a lander's choice", which quoted pre-D94 S2; red-team cycle 2 round 5, XS-R4-STALE-S2-QUOTE, 2026-10-04). The opening-subrun projection and the `lastVCHash` writer S2-R15 step 3 needs stay the W15 row 'C2-R05/R06' (owner adoption); no new slot, event or field is introduced here.

**Failure behaviour [assumed: stated from the rule text, no behaviour added]:** no failure path of its own; the rule is definitional. Its writes run only on acceptance, as L10, after L1 to L9 have passed (C2-R05), and L11 follows them, except the checkpoint or provisional record, which follows L11 and is decided from its `zkLeaves_` (revised 2026-10-04, L1-01); a revert of any check, L11 included, rolls every write of this rule back by transaction atomicity (C2-R01: "the proof's revert rolls back every write"; C2-R15: the checkpoint call "is rolled back by" the proof's revert). So a reverted landing leaves `lastLanded`, the term records, the forced-inclusion state, the checkpoint map, the provisional ring and every ledger as they were. Vectors: E03 and E11 (the writes on acceptance), E04 (one revert per check, with state before and after).

## 5. Arbitration, replacement and the horizon

### C2-R07. Deadlines are economic

**[assumed: proposed rule]** `deadline(t) = termEnd(t) + LAND_WINDOW` is where the ramps of C2-R16 reach their maximum for a term landable from its end; LAND_WINDOW_MAX caps the lag chained late landings build inside A1. The clock voids nothing and no rule penalizes a term for not landing (invariants I4, I5). Round 4 removed the abandonment penalty with its evidence deadline, outage gate, void waiver and escrow (R4H-5, R4-C3): a certified view is landable by anyone and paid from the holder's reserve, so a holder cannot abandon a provable view; the only unlandable certified content is a guest bug, a false positive for any abandonment rule, handled by replacement, up to C3-R06's gate; VALID_GOSSIP request blocks are landable (C3-R06's landability claim, D41). (Landing §6.)

**Failure behaviour [assumed: stated from the rule text, no behaviour added]:** no failure path; the rule is definitional. `deadline(t)` is a ramp parameter of C2-R16 and no check of C2-R05 reads it (L2's `deadline` is the lander's own calldata field, a different value), so passing `deadline(t)` refuses no landing and voids no block. The time-based refusals that exist are stated elsewhere: C2-R08's A1 gate on replacements and hatches and C2-R10's horizon. Vector: E14 (§14).

### C2-R08. A1, replaceability, and the landing announcement

**[assumed: proposed rule]** With `t'` the first unlanded term,

```text
replaceableFrom = min( termEnd(t') + LAND_WINDOW_MAX,
                       max( termEnd(t') + LAND_WINDOW, lastLanded.at + LAND_CHAIN_GRACE ) ) + REPLACE_GRACE
                  + LAND_CHAIN_GRACE · [deferral flag spent by an announcement]
                  (deferral term written out 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 4, L1-R4-01; earlier stated in prose only)
```

A replacement landing (C2-R05 L4's `isReplace`, dead mode (b) and the D40(3) sentinel disjunct (c) included; CS-R4-01, 2026-10-04; (c) added red-team cycle 2 round 4 second recheck, CS-R4-01 (D40(3)), 2026-10-04) is accepted iff `block.timestamp > replaceableFrom`; inputs are inbox storage and the block timestamp. A hatch (C3-R07) is accepted on that A1 disjunct only if also `block.timestamp ≥ hatchAt + HATCH_RESUME_GRACE`, or in an open window of the current request (C3-R07 check 1), while replacement and dead mode keep A1 as is; a non-maximal hatch waits HATCH_RESUME_GRACE after any hatch on either disjunct (C3-R07 (4a)). Anyone with a TAIKO ledger balance may call `announceLanding(firstHeight, endHeight)` with `firstHeight = lastLanded.height + 1` while `replaceableFrom` has not passed, locking ANNOUNCE_BOND on the ledger; it defers `replaceableFrom` by LAND_CHAIN_GRACE once per range, through a single flag keyed on `lastLanded` with three values: unspent; spent by an announcement, the only value that defers; and consumed by an accepted REPLACE or RESUME record on an unspent flag, which defers nothing and leaves no deferral for a later announcement; a record accepted while the flag is already spent by an announcement leaves it spent, so the announcement's deferral stays in force and `replaceableFrom` does not change **[proven: from the formula; the deferral term is LAND_CHAIN_GRACE before and after]** (earlier: "and consumed by an accepted REPLACE or RESUME record", unconditional, so a record after an announcement read as spent → consumed dropped `replaceableFrom` by 600 s with no checkpoint; red-team cycle 2 round 5, L1-R5-01, 2026-10-04; E23 (iv)) (round 5, R5-C3; C3's 112-minute bound relies on this being one deferral) **[assumed: proposed rule; revised 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 4, L1-R4-01]** (earlier "through a single flag keyed on `lastLanded` spent by whichever of an announcement and an accepted REPLACE or RESUME record comes first", which §14 (c), C2-R18 and S2-V47 (c) read as adding LAND_CHAIN_GRACE for a record too; since `recordViewChange` accepts a record only when `block.timestamp > replaceableFrom` (C2-R09), every accepted record then pushed `replaceableFrom` back above the current time, re-closing A1 for up to 600 s for free, so the fork's landing reverted `NotReplaceable` at L9 and an A1 hatch `HatchNotPermitted`; vector E23 (iv)). **[proven: from the formula and C2-R09's record gate]** A record leaves `replaceableFrom` unchanged and A1 true; only a bonded announcement, made before `replaceableFrom` passes, defers it. An ordinary landing covering `firstHeight` returns the bond; a hatch or replacement landing (`isReplace`, C2-R05 L4) first forfeits it, 40 % to that lander and 60 % burned (a pre-A1 window hatch included, RE-2 Low 2, C3 §11) (revised 2026-10-04, CS-R4-02: "replacement" was read from the lander-set `replace`, so an ordinary landing of the announced range relabelled a replacement took 40 % of an honest announcer's bond; E23 (ii)); a CONFLICT restart voids every outstanding announcement and returns its bond, and `announceLanding` in CONFLICT reverts `NotActive` (C2-R14; added 2026-10-04, L1-R4-02). An announcement is refused once the head forced entry has been due at the first unlanded term's T_floor for longer than LAND_WINDOW (round 3, C3). **[open: this rule rests on C3 at `4aa0299`; D39 withdraws due-ness, so its input under D41 is undefined until C2's follow-up, §16 item 17]** Nodes label confirmations above `lastLanded` "replaceable" from `replaceableFrom` (S2). **The checkpoint ring (revision 2026-10-04, SL-2) [assumed: proposed rule]:** the first call in an L1 block that changes an input of `replaceableFrom`, on any path, first writes `rfLog[block.number mod 8,192] = (block.number, rfLogHead, replaceableFrom)` and sets `rfLogHead = block.number`; later calls in that block write none. The rule binds every writer of an input, not a list of entry points; the writers known are a landing that moves `lastLanded`, an announcement that spends the deferral flag (an accepted REPLACE or RESUME record is no writer: it moves the flag from unspent to consumed, both giving the deferral term 0, and leaves a spent flag spent, the term LAND_CHAIN_GRACE before and after, so in either case `replaceableFrom`'s value does not change **[proven: from the formula]** (the spent case added red-team cycle 2 round 5, L1-R5-01, 2026-10-04); revised 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 4, L1-R4-01, earlier "an announcement or accepted REPLACE or RESUME record that spends the deferral flag"), a DAO change of LAND_WINDOW, LAND_WINDOW_MAX, LAND_CHAIN_GRACE or REPLACE_GRACE, C4-R12's restart and the CONFLICT reinitializer (which rewind `lastLanded`, set `lastLanded.at = restartTimestamp`, reset `lastLandedTerm` and clear the deferral flag; the checkpoint precedes those writes), and any upgrade initializer that changes a grace, including one whose graces are bytecode constants, so the upgrade itself changes them (revised 2026-10-04, SL-2-R1: the earlier parenthesis listed only the first three, so an implementer hooking those paths left the restart unrecorded, and a pre-restart REPLACE at `N < K` read the post-restart value on the `M = 0` branch, framing an honest voter or letting a premature one escape; vector S3-V05 (SL-2-R1); the C4-R12 half is a proposal to B, §16). **[proven, conditional on that writer rule holding on every path, upgrade initializers included (open for C4-R12: B's adoption, §16): `replaceableFrom` is a function of storage and constants only, so it changes only at checkpointed writes]** its value at the start of an L1 block N inside EIP-2935's window is the entry's `rfBefore` at the one checkpoint block `M` with `prevBlock < N ≤ M`, or the live value when `rfLogHead < N`; `M ≤ block.number < N + 8,192`, so the ring still holds M. S3d reads it (S3-R08); C3-R09's "before that block's landings" is this start-of-block value. Cost: one ring write and one head write per such block, **unmeasured**. (Landing §6 A1; round 2, S3; round 4 renamed ABANDON_GRACE to REPLACE_GRACE.) **Adversary schedule:** a fake announcement costs ANNOUNCE_BOND and delays the A1 hatch by at most one grace period; a gossiped REPLACE or RESUME VC recorded by anyone delays nothing (L1-R4-01); a landable backlog is never reorged while someone bonded is landing it.

### C2-R09. A3, landing-based L1 arbitration

**[assumed: proposed rule]** Until a replacement or hatch lands, landing the original stays valid and pays the ramp; whichever lands first wins, and the loser is invalid and free under the primary shape (C5-R12's shape 2, C2-R20). A recorded REPLACE or RESUME VC has no closing effect: `recordViewChange` accepts one only if `vc.lockHeight == lastLanded.height`, `vc.lockPhHash == lastLanded.phHash` and `block.timestamp > replaceableFrom` at record time, and the record serves only (i) as the first-recorded VC for `(t, v, closes)` that the fork's landing must match under L4 and (ii) as priority evidence for the A1 path, consuming C2-R08's single deferral flag without deferring if it is unspent, so that no later announcement defers and `replaceableFrom` does not move, and leaving it spent if an announcement already spent it, so the deferral stays in force and `replaceableFrom` again does not move (red-team cycle 2 round 5, L1-R5-01, 2026-10-04: the spent case; earlier "consuming C2-R08's single deferral flag without deferring", unconditional) (revised 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 4, L1-R4-01; earlier "spending the single deferral of C2-R08", which added LAND_CHAIN_GRACE and so re-closed the A1 the record had to satisfy); `recordViewChange` keeps this A1 gate for RESUME records, and a RESUME after a pre-A1 window hatch lands through the view-change chain unrecorded (C3-R09). The record is dead once `lastLanded.height > vc.lockHeight`: the first-recorded rule ignores it, so the original view's own TIMEOUT or TERM_END VC can still be recorded or landed, and it is no longer priority evidence. The fork's opening object (a REPLACE VC carrying an L1 reference) and the attester conditions are S2's (A2); a RESUME lock vote's `l1Ref` is the `(number, hash)` of the L1 block containing the replacement, dead-mode or hatch landing resumed from, and a RESUME is attestable iff C3-R09's one predicate holds: the hash is canonical at that number, and either A1 held at `l1Ref`, evaluated on the inbox state before that block's landings, or `hatchTip[l1Ref.number] == keccak256(abi.encode(lockHeight, lockPhHash))`, both taken from the signed vote; a REPLACE keeps A1 only (C3-R09; in-tree in [S2](S2-certificates-and-handoff.md) under D78: S2-R09 makes `l1Ref` nonzero only for REPLACE and RESUME and S2-R15 binds REPLACE to A1 and RESUME to C3-R09's predicate, with S2-V34's fixtures; revised 2026-10-03 at the S2/S3 adoption, earlier "S2-R09's and S2-R15's matching text is **pending S2**"). The ring of the interfaces page (`forcedRecord(uint8)`, FORCED_RING) is replaced by `hatchTip`, `mapping(uint64 => bytes32)`, keyed by L1 block number, value `keccak256(abi.encode(uint64 endHeight, bytes32 endPhHash))` of the hatch's end, written by a hatch, a replacement or a dead-mode landing (the last two being C2-R05 L4's `isReplace` by (a) or (b), which a lander cannot set on an ordinary landing; CS-R4-02, 2026-10-04; a landing `isReplace` by (c) only writes no marker, red-team cycle 2 round 4 second recheck, CS-R4-01 (D40(3)), 2026-10-04; S1-R17; open-empty and no-committee view-255 landings do not establish the marker, since they do not set `hatchL1`, but one that follows a qualifying landing in the same L1 block updates its tip under C3-R06 step 7; revised 2026-10-03 for B's Low on `982aac6`, earlier "do not write it") and rewritten by every later landing in that L1 block (C3-R06 step 7 as revised 2026-10-03, C3-R07), so an honest RESUME from any of the three passes the hatchTip disjunct even when C2-R08's deferral, reset by an earlier ordinary landing in the same block, made A1 false on the state before that block's landings (C3 T50) **[proven: C3-R07 claim, for the hatchTip write and the attestability it gives; open: whether a replacement or dead-mode landing can follow a deferral-resetting ordinary landing in the same L1 block, since its opening object must name that landing's end, C3-R07; relabelled 2026-10-03 on red team recheck, the label read "[proven: C3-R07 claim]"]** (slashing-05; earlier: "rewritten by every later landing in that L1 block" after a hatch only). Dead-mode landings (view 255, no certificate) are replacements (C2-R05 L4's `isReplace` (b)) and obey A1; so are post-deadline landings and open-empty or holder-path landings whose S1-R16 scan finds a certificate-mode term (`isReplace` (c), D40(3); added red-team cycle 2 round 4 second recheck, CS-R4-01 (D40(3)), 2026-10-04). A hatch voids every certified block above `lastLanded` (C3-R09), and a window hatch does so before A1 (D51(4)). A maximal hatch moves `base` so that no forced-inclusion window opens before `hatchAt + HATCH_RESUME_GRACE` (C3-R04); a non-maximal hatch waits HATCH_RESUME_GRACE after any hatch on either disjunct and extends the head's close instead of moving `base` (C3-R07 (4a)); so after a maximal hatch every hatch waits HATCH_RESUME_GRACE, and after a non-maximal one only a maximal hatch may come sooner (C3-R09). (Landing §6 A3; round 4, R4H-5 removed round 3's recorded-REPLACE close, a proof-free void, and R4-C3 restored "a proving outage voids nothing".)

Two landings of one range, by one lander or two, carry the same certified content and may differ only in the signature witness and the quorum witness bytes of the view changes they carry (C2-R02; D21, D23); their journals share every field but `blobHashesRoot` and `rewardTo`, and the first included wins as before: the second fails L1 (`NotNext`) and is free under the primary shape. A reused proof (C2-R02) is submitted with its bound recipient and its bound blobs; a submitter who is not the recipient pays the gas and the recipient is paid, and two submissions of one proof race on L1 ordering like any two landings. (B's W8 verdict on #22196 (2026-10-01), B-C2-01 and B-C2-02.)

**[proven: replacement is locally decidable]** A1 is a predicate on inbox storage and the block timestamp; attester disagreements resolve by L1 ordering of landings, since only a landing moves `lastLanded`. **[proven: why descendants are void]** Every certified block above `lastLanded` was built on a state L1 will never hold once a replacement lands; its proof asserts a parent root the inbox does not store.

### C2-R10. The landing horizon, the one time-based void

**[assumed: proposed rule]** The landing horizon is the smaller of ROLE_HORIZON (S1, 34 h) and L1's EIP-2935 window (8,191 L1 blocks: 27 h at 12-s slots, 4.55 h at 2-s slots). A range whose first term is older than ROLE_HORIZON and was never pinned, or whose anchor tip has left the window, can no longer be landed (L4, L7) and is replaceable (L4 reverts `TermBeyondHorizon` for the never-pinned term, older meaning `block.timestamp > termStart(t) + ROLE_HORIZON`, the cutoff of S1-R14's `recordAssignment` window and of S1-R09's retained suspension history; L7 reverts `AnchorTooOld`; **[assumed: arbiter answer, D72, revised by the arbiter's ruling on B-W18-A01]**); its certified and locked blocks are lost without a slash (L14; round 1, F7 found "nothing voids by clock" overstated). Nodes label confirmations "outage" once no landing that updates `lastLanded.at` has happened for DEGRADE_AFTER (S2). A proving outage shorter than the horizon voids nothing; landing after it resumes is conditional on someone still holding the bytes, which C2-R18 guarantees only for RETAIN_SECONDS (a limitation, §16). This holds for a one-back-end outage too, but single-proof mode does not extend it: one-leaf landings advance at most 240 blocks per DEGRADE_AFTER, 240 s of chain only under full production (C2-R12's throughput bound; B-W22-A05, 2026-10-03, earlier "at most 240 s of chain"), so, at full L2 production (C2-R12's premise), the horizon is still reached, measured from the oldest unlanded block's anchor tip, about 4.7 h after the outage starts at 2-s L1 slots and about 28 h at 12-s slots with every L1 slot filled and at full L2 production, C2-R12's full-cadence premise [assumed: premise; qualified 2026-10-03, the `ca4a942` follow-up Lows]; sparser L2 production lets a 240-block landing cover more elapsed time, so the horizon comes later or not at all, retention past RETAIN_SECONDS having already left landing to voluntary holders (L25), and the backlog is then lost as above (L-SPT, proposed, §16) [proven: arithmetic in C2-R12; added 2026-10-03, finding landing-02]. Vector: E15 (§14).

### C2-R11. Certificate-free landings always need two leaves

**[assumed: proposed rule]** A landing whose last segment is a sentinel view (dead, no-committee, open-empty) or a hatch (view 254) is accepted with an empty certificate and never in single-proof mode: `kRequired` stays ZK_K; with fewer than ZK_K accepting leaves it reverts `BadProof` at L11 **[assumed: arbiter answer, D72]**. One back-end outage in such a regime stalls it until the DAO rotates images or adds a leaf (L19; round 3, H4): without an attester-executed state root, one proof system would carry state safety alone. Vector: E16 (§14).

### C2-R20. Landing shapes and confirmation levels

**[assumed: proposed rule]** The transaction layouts are C5-R12's, its one normative statement, cited and not restated here: the primary shape is C5-R12's four-frame shape 2, direct to builder under the lander's own nonce, in which any revert of the frame calling `land` (lost race, `NotNext`, bad proof, rotated image, out of gas) makes the transaction invalid, no gas and no blob fee; the public-mempool fallback is C5-R12's shape 1 through the shared-nonce gate G_LAND calling `landFor`, deployed once EIP-8141 is Final, inert while its immutable address is zero (`landFor` then reverts `NotLandGate` for every caller, C5-R14; **[assumed: arbiter answer, D72]**), free only against same-nonce losers; without EIP-8141, C5-R12's direct land, a type-3 transaction with `deadline` (L2, `DeadlineExceeded`) whose loser reverts and pays C5-R15's D-FLOOR(L) for a reverted direct land plus the blob fees of its own blobs; C2-R20 cites C5-R15 for that cost and quotes no separate figure **[assumed: arbiter answer, D72]**. C5 owns the gate (its pins C5-R13, its address and lifecycle C5-R14). (D31: the three-frame layout of earlier drafts lacked the lander's approval frame and was invalid, B-C5-01.) Levels, none in slots: **landed** = in an L1 block the node has, checkpoint written, or "landed (provisional)" while an unfinalized provisional record is on its chain; **L1-safe** = LANDED_CONFIRM_DEPTH descendants; **L1-final** = finalized per the node's beacon client, reported, never consumed by a contract; **void** = replaced or removed by an L1 reorg, inbox state reverting with L1, the range returning to CERTIFIED, no penalty. An ePBS Empty slot leaves the transaction unexecuted; the expiry frame makes the stale copy free. (Landing §8; frame-transactions page; after the #22188 review.) Vector: E19 (§14).

## 6. Checkpoint write, single-proof mode and provisional finality

### C2-R12. Single-proof mode

**[assumed: proposed rule]** If `block.timestamp − lastLanded.at > DEGRADE_AFTER`, a landing with a non-empty certificate is accepted with `kRequired = 1` (`SingleProofMode(true)`); if it is accepted with one leaf, it is provisional (C2-R13); "accepted with one leaf" means L11's returned `zkLeaves_` names exactly one distinct ZK leaf, whatever was presented, and a landing in the mode with two accepting leaves writes the checkpoint (C2-R15) (clarified 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 2, L1-01). A landing that updates `lastLanded.at` (every landing but a hatch: ordinary, sentinel, dead-mode and replacement, C2-R06) ends the mode and resets the timer; the next landing needs ZK_K leaves until DEGRADE_AFTER has passed again. A hatch preserves `lastLanded.at` (C2-R06, C3-R07), so it neither ends the mode nor resets the timer: with the last timer-updating landing at 0 and a valid hatch at 8,000 (> DEGRADE_AFTER = 7,200), a certified landing at 8,001 still qualifies for one leaf (B's W8 on #22218, comment 5952257755, item 1). One-leaf landings are still at least DEGRADE_AFTER apart, because a one-leaf landing is certified, never a hatch (which always needs ZK_K leaves, C2-R11), so it updates `lastLanded.at` and restarts the timer. (Landing §7, §10; round 3, H4; made provisional after the #22188 review.) **Adversary schedule:** the mode cannot be opened by an adversary while a funded lander can obtain ZK_K proofs (every required leaf available, the bytes retained per C2-R18, a certificate in hand, inclusion on L1), since any landing that updates `lastLanded.at` resets the clock (a hatch does not, but that funded lander's own certified landing does); it opens exactly when one of those conditions fails for DEGRADE_AFTER, which is its purpose, so with ZK_K = 2 one unavailable leaf opens it while the other leaf works; a timer cannot tell which condition failed, and the provisional record (C2-R13) is what bounds the consequence (L-PF); an idle chain enters it harmlessly, its one-leaf landing being provisional. (B's W8 verdict on #22196 (2026-10-01), B-C2-04, which found the earlier "nobody can trigger the mode while any leaf works" false at ZK_K = 2.)

**[proven: single-proof throughput bound, arithmetic on §12's values; added 2026-10-03, finding landing-02]** One-leaf landings are at least DEGRADE_AFTER apart (above), and each carries at most MAX_VIEWS = 4 segments and MAX_BLOCKS = 256 blocks (C2-R05 L1, L4). Every segment lies inside one term: a holder view lies inside one term, and a sentinel-255 block carries the term containing its own `PH.timestamp` (C7-R02, C7-R06), so a sentinel chain is one segment per term (C2-R02's maximal `(termId, view)` runs; C2-R05 L4; S1-R16's landable prefix). A term holds at most TERM = 60 blocks (C7-R08), so any one-leaf landing carries at most min(256, 4 × 60) = 240 blocks (MAX_BLOCKS = 256 does not bind). Those blocks lie in at most four occupied terms, which need not be consecutive, because a term with no block has no segment (C2-R02), so no bound on elapsed chain time follows; under full production, every term holding blocks, the four segments are four consecutive terms, 240 s of chain (corrected 2026-10-03, B-W22-A05; the text read "at most 240 s of chain"). The figures below assume full production, as their premise states. At the 1-s cadence the chain produces up to 7,200 blocks per DEGRADE_AFTER. So while the mode lasts, certified landing advances by at most 240/7,200, one thirtieth, of production, and the unlanded backlog grows by at least 6,960 s of chain per DEGRADE_AFTER at full cadence. If L4 admits only one sentinel segment per landing (S1-R16's open point) the sentinel share is lower still. **Corrected 2026-10-03 (red team round 1 recheck, cross-section-02 consistency) [proven: from C7-R02, C7-R06, C2-R02 and S1-R16 as they stand]:** this paragraph first read "a one-leaf landing whose segments are all holder views carries at most min(256, 4 × 60) = 240 blocks, and any one-leaf landing carries at most MAX_BLOCKS = 256 blocks (a non-final sentinel-255 segment may span terms, C7-R06; C2-R11 bars only a sentinel last segment), at most 256 s of chain", with 256/7,200 (about one twenty-eighth) and 6,944 s; the span-terms premise contradicts C7-R06 and S1-R16 and is withdrawn, and the figures below are recomputed at 240 s; the horizon times and landing counts are unchanged. More views per term (TIMEOUT view changes) only lower the landed share. **[proven: consequence, arithmetic on C2-R08 and C2-R10, at full cadence, with every L1 slot filled (L7 counts 8,191 blocks; missed slots lengthen the window in time), and each range's anchor tip at least ANCHOR_MIN_AGE old]** Take the last timer-updating landing at time 0 and both leaves needed thereafter unavailable but one. The k-th one-leaf landing comes no earlier than 7,200·k s and, under full production, ends no later than block 240·k s, so its anchor tip is at least 6,960·k + 48 s old; L7 refuses it once that exceeds the EIP-2935 window (C2-R10). At 2-s L1 slots (16,382 s) at most two one-leaf landings succeed and the next unlanded block's anchor leaves the window about 4.7 h after time 0; at 12-s slots (98,292 s) at most fourteen succeed, about 28 h. From then the backlog is unlandable, replaceable, and its certified and locked blocks are lost without a slash (L14). Retention binds earlier [proven: arithmetic on C2-R18 and §12]: RETAIN_SECONDS = 7,200 s, so from the second one-leaf landing on, the bytes it needs are older than the serving duty, and landing them depends on a voluntary holder (L25, C2-R10's limitation). The horizon times above are therefore latest points of loss, not guaranteed landings. Before that, A1 holds throughout the mode: after each one-leaf landing `replaceableFrom` is about `termEnd(t′) + 3,900 s` (C2-R08) with `t′` only about four terms past the previous landed end, earlier than the next one-leaf opportunity, so every later range of the backlog races replacements (C2-R09). So single-proof mode keeps landing from halting, but it does not keep certified operation landed: during a one-back-end outage the backlog is replaceable from A1 (C2-R08), unguaranteed past RETAIN_SECONDS (L25) and void at the landing horizon (C2-R10), which is the limitation L-SPT (§16), proposed 2026-10-03 and signed by D79 (1) on 2026-10-04. **[assumed: why the spacing stays]** The spacing is what bounds the provisional ring (C2-R13's ring bound, PROVISIONAL_RING = 12); relaxing it re-derives PROVISIONAL_RING, the record gas of §12 and L-PF's rollback span, a design change this revision does not make. **[signed: D79 (1), L-SPT]** (2026-10-04: L-SPT signed as an exception to R1 while a proof system is down, the split-timer design kept as a research track for a later DAO upgrade; earlier "[open: user sign-off, L-SPT]", label unified 2026-10-03 on A's recheck, earlier "[open: user decision, L-SPT]") The question was: either sign L-SPT as an exception to R1, or open a design round for a faster single-proof rule (for example, a mode timer keyed on the last landing accepted with ZK_K leaves rather than on `lastLanded.at`, with a shorter spacing between one-leaf landings and a re-derived ring). **Design round 2026-10-03 [assumed: review record; open: research]:** that round was run after B's review of `982aac6` ("I see no demonstrated impossibility forcing L-SPT acceptance"; "a timer-only change retaining 12 records is unsafe"). Its split-timer candidate (outage entry from the last landing accepted with ZK_K leaves, one-leaf spacing, a resized ring with a capped finalization pass and explicit 24 h boundaries, longer retention, and held one-leaf credits) was attacked twice and found unsound as proposed: under byte load a landing's LANDING_BYTES = 390,132 bytes against V10's 1,270,000 bytes per term gives about 0.154× production at a 120 s spacing **[proven: arithmetic]**, and the fixes for the ring restart, the held money, the mode's stickiness and a CONFLICT front-run each need a new, unattacked rule. L-SPT therefore stays the disclosed limitation, and the candidate is a research note ([single-proof split timer](../research/single-proof-split-timer.md)); the user's choice is unchanged. **[proven: from C2-R02; earlier "[open: whether a term with no blocks needs a segment]", revised 2026-10-03, B-W22-A05]** A term with no blocks has no segment, so a one-leaf landing may span more than four terms of time when production is sparse; the 240-block bound holds either way, and sequencers that slowed production toward the landed rate would land a larger share of a smaller chain, which this page does not assume.

**Failure behaviour [assumed: stated from the rule text, no behaviour added]:** the rule has no check of its own; it sets the `kRequired` that L11 passes to `verify`. A landing that presents fewer distinct accepting ZK leaves than its `kRequired` (one inside the mode with a non-empty certificate; ZK_K outside the mode, and always for a certificate-free landing, C2-R11) fails L11: `verify` reverts (C2-R03) and every write rolls back (C2-R01). The revert is `BadProof` (C2-R03, §10) **[assumed: arbiter answer, D72]**. Vectors: E06 and E12 (one-leaf landings inside the mode), E16 (one leaf presented by a certificate-free landing inside the mode, and the hatch that leaves the timer running).

### C2-R13. Provisional records, confirmation and finalization

**[assumed: proposed rule]** A one-leaf landing advances `lastLanded`, pays rewards and runs every deadline as a normal landing, but writes no checkpoint. It stores a `ProvisionalRecord` (§10), whose `leafId` is the one accepting leaf L11 returned (C2-R05 L11; added 2026-10-04, L1-01), in a ring of PROVISIONAL_RING slots keyed `k % PROVISIONAL_RING`; the start fields are `lastLanded`'s height, block hash and state root, the forced-inclusion head at the landing, and `startDigest = keccak256(abi.encode(lastLanded, lastVCHash))` as the landing found them (fix pass of 2026-10-01: what a checkpoint and a rollback are taken from; the digest after B's W12 verdict on #22196, so that C2-R14's restart restores the PH and opening identity from storage). The record becomes CONFIRMED when anyone presents, through `confirmProvisional(k, recorded, journal, proof)`, the record's journal `recorded` (which must hash to its `journalHash`) and a valid proof from another leaf of a journal equal to it in every field (no reward; the lander was paid at landing); `confirmProvisional` calls `verifier.verify(keccak256(abi.encode(journal)), proof, 1)` and requires the returned `zkLeaves_` to name a ZK leaf other than `record.leafId`, else `NotConflict`; on confirmation the record stores `confirmingLeaf`, the lowest ZK leaf id in the returned set other than `record.leafId` **[assumed: proposed rule; added red-team cycle 2 round 5, L1-R5-02, 2026-10-04; open: the id-set encoding, C8 §3.1 question 33]** (earlier the confirming leaf was not stored, so a later pass could not tell leaf 1 from leaf 2 at ZK_N = 3); "another leaf" is judged from those accepting leaves, and presented tags (`Proof.leafId`, C8 §6) count for nothing, as at L11 **[assumed: proposed rule; open: C8 §3.1 question 33's encoding]** (added 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 2, L1-01-R1; earlier "a valid proof from another leaf" named neither the `kRequired` nor how the leaf is judged); a journal equal in every fixed input and different in a derived output (C2-R04's partition, `fiBlocksMatch = false` among such differences since landing-01, 2026-10-03) that also passes C2-R14's queue checks (c) is C2-R14's conflict instead, and any other journal reverts `NotConflict`, and finalizable when DEGRADED_FINALITY elapse after `landedAt` with no conflict. Finalization, run first by every landing and by `finalizeProvisional(k)` alone, passes the records in order from the oldest while each is CONFIRMED or older than DEGRADED_FINALITY (and, in CONFLICT, has an index below `conflictK`, C2-R14; added 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 3, L1-R3-01) and writes one checkpoint: the start of the oldest record still unfinalized, or `lastLanded` when none remains; `ProvisionalFinalized(k, confirmingLeaf)` carries the record's stored `confirmingLeaf` for a CONFIRMED record, even one also older than DEGRADED_FINALITY, and `type(uint8).max` = 255 only for a PROVISIONAL record finalized by elapsed time, a value outside the leaf-id range (ZK_N ≤ 3) **[assumed: proposed rule; revised 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 4, L1-R4-03; the stored source and the CONFIRMED-and-old case red-team cycle 2 round 5, L1-R5-02, 2026-10-04, earlier "carries `type(uint8).max` = 255 for the time rule", with no stored input for the leaf]** (earlier "carries leaf 0 for the time rule", byte-identical to a confirmation by ZK leaf 0, a real leaf id in E13 and E12 (viii), so an indexer could not tell a two-system confirmation from a single proof plus elapsed time; vector E23 (vi)). A record behind an unfinalized one waits; a pass that finalizes no record writes nothing. Neither is a revert, so no error names them, and `ProvisionalPending` and `NotFinalizable` are withdrawn from §10 **[assumed: arbiter answer, D72]**. A later landing from a provisional end state, with one leaf or two, confirms nothing: a sound proof "from S to S′" says nothing about whether S was true. Checkpoints are written in order. (Landing §7; after the #22188 review and its fix pass.)

**[assumed: proposed rule; added 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 3, L1-R3-01, L1-R3-02] Indices, cursor and preconditions.** Records are numbered `k = 0, 1, 2, …` (`uint32`) in landing order. The cursor word (C8-R15 slot 278) holds `first`, the index of the oldest record that is not FINAL (equal to `next` when there is none); `next`, the index the next one-leaf landing stores; the CONFLICT flag; and `conflictK`, the lowest CONFLICTED index, meaningful only while the flag is set (C2-R14). The ring bound below gives `next − first ≤ PROVISIONAL_RING`, so slot `k % PROVISIONAL_RING` holds record `k` exactly when `first ≤ k < next`. `confirmProvisional(k, …)` first runs the finalization pass above, as a landing does (in CONFLICT the pass stops before `conflictK`), and then checks in this order, against the cursor and statuses the pass leaves **[assumed: proposed rule; added 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 3 Lows; earlier "`confirmProvisional(k, …)` checks in this order." with no pass, so a record older than DEGRADED_FINALITY that no landing or `finalizeProvisional` had yet passed was still PROVISIONAL at check (1) and could be contested]**. (1) `first ≤ k < next` and record `k`'s status is PROVISIONAL, else `NotConflict`. (2) `recorded` hashes to `journalHash`, else `NotConflict`. (3) In CONFLICT, C2-R14's admission rule applies, else `NotActive`. (4) The `verify` and leaf checks above, with the confirm or conflict predicate, else `NotConflict`. Check (1) covers both the confirm path and the conflict path. Because the pass runs first, a record that is time-finalizable when the call executes is FINAL at check (1), and both paths revert `NotConflict`; the revert also undoes the call's own pass, which changes no outcome, since the record is refused either way and the next landing or `finalizeProvisional` finalizes it. The pass adds its gas, unmeasured as at a landing, to every `confirmProvisional` (2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 3 Lows). Earlier, neither path named a status or index precondition, and only §11's state machine drew both edges from PROVISIONAL. **[assumed: proposed rule; the alternative is recorded]** CONFIRMED and FINAL records are therefore not contestable. The finding's alternative, making CONFIRMED records contestable, is not taken. **[proven: why nothing sound is lost, conditional on T3 holding for at least one of the two ZK leaves (ZK_N = 2)]** Suppose a record is CONFIRMED and a conflict on it is valid. Then the confirming leaf, the only ZK leaf other than `record.leafId`, has accepted both `recorded` and a `J′` with the same fixed inputs, so that leaf alone is unsound (C2-R14's partition claim). By T3 the record's own leaf is then sound and the record is true, so accepting the conflict would only let one unsound leaf halt the chain in normal two-leaf mode, the case ZK_K = 2 is meant to make harmless (§12). A FINAL record has already published its checkpoint, and C4-R12's floor keeps it. A late conflict could not void that checkpoint; it could only halt the chain, and C2-R14's restart would have no unfinalized record `r` to restore from. **[assumed: proposed; pending user sign-off] (the FINAL half; added 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 3 Lows)** Whether a proven-false FINAL root should halt the chain or only be recorded is a policy choice; the **[proven]** claim above covers CONFIRMED records only. As proposed, a late counterproof against a FINAL record is an off-chain alarm only: `confirmProvisional` reverts `NotConflict`, nothing is recorded or emitted on chain, and the chain keeps landing on the state the counterproof shows false; the DAO may act on the alarm by upgrade. The alternative, an event without a halt, would need a non-reverting path and an event C8 does not have. C6's L-PF row records it. A record that is false and unconfirmed must still be contested within DEGRADED_FINALITY, which is L-PF as before; `confirmProvisional`'s own pass enforces that deadline (2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 3 Lows; earlier the sentence stood with no check enforcing it).

**[proven: ring bound]** One-leaf landings are at least DEGRADE_AFTER apart (C2-R12) and a record older than DEGRADED_FINALITY without conflict is finalizable, so at most DEGRADED_FINALITY / DEGRADE_AFTER = 12 records are unfinalized at once, the oldest finalizable before a thirteenth is appended; conditional on finalization running, which every landing does first.

**[proven: why the record stores its start]** With a record's own end as the checkpoint, a two-leaf landing just after record k − 1 waited for record k (up to 23 h later) to finalize, about two windows, and a conflict on k rolled back two windows, beyond ROLE_HORIZON. With the start stored, the newest checkpoint is the start of the oldest unfinalized record: a two-leaf landing waits at most one DEGRADED_FINALITY, a conflict voids at most one (C2-R14), the two-leaf landings between records are covered by the next record's start, and a newer checkpoint proves every signal an older one did.

### C2-R14. CONFLICT and the DAO resolution

**[assumed: proposed rule]** A conflict on record `k` is a valid proof from a leaf other than the record's (judged as in C2-R13: `verify(keccak256(abi.encode(J′)), proof, 1)` returns `zkLeaves_` naming a ZK leaf other than `record.leafId`, presented tags counting for nothing, else `NotConflict`; `ProofConflict`'s `leafB` is that accepting leaf **[assumed: proposed rule; open: C8 §3.1 question 33's encoding]**; added 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 2, L1-01-R1), presented through `confirmProvisional(k, recorded, journal, proof)` with `recorded` hashing to the record's `journalHash`, over a journal `J′` that (a) equals `recorded` in every fixed input of C2-R04's partition; (b) differs from it in at least one derived output, every derived output being free to differ, alone or together; and (c) passes the queue checks against immutable storage that make its derived outputs functions of its fixed inputs; under D41 every forced-inclusion field is a fixed input (C2-R04), so (a) already makes them equal to `recorded`'s, which passed C3-R06 steps 1 and 3 at landing, and (c) holds (C3-R06's claim). The landing's other checks (L1, L4, L5, L7 and the gate of C3-R06 step 2) are not re-run: they decide what may land, and a true journal that would fail one still contradicts the record. The inbox records the conflict, emits `ProofConflict(k, leafA, leafB)` and enters CONFLICT. Recording it sets record `k`'s status to CONFLICTED and the cursor word's `conflictK` to `k` (C8-R06, C8-R15 slot 278; added 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 3, L1-R3-01). In CONFLICT there is no landing of either mode, the hatch and `fiClear` included (C3-R06a, C3-R07). There is also no checkpoint write except by the finalization pass over records with index below `conflictK`, run by `finalizeProvisional` or at the start of a `confirmProvisional` call (a successful conflict on an earlier record can finalize an aged predecessor and write its checkpoint; B's W8 verdict on #22250, nonblocking; earlier "except by `finalizeProvisional`"), which writes at most record `conflictK`'s start. Both hold until a DAO upgrade names the sound leaf. (Earlier: "no checkpoint write except by `finalizeProvisional` of records preceding the conflicting one, which writes at most that record's start". Only a bool flag was stored, so the conflicting index had no stored input.) **[assumed: proposed rule; added 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 3, L1-R3-01] A later conflict on an earlier record.** In CONFLICT, `confirmProvisional(k′, …)` is admitted only as a conflict on a record `k′ < conflictK` that passes C2-R13's checks (1) and (2) and this rule's predicate. It sets record `k′` to CONFLICTED, lowers `conflictK` to `k′` and emits `ProofConflict(k′, leafA, leafB)`. On a PROVISIONAL record `first ≤ k′ < next`, which passes check (1), the confirm path and a conflict at `k′ > conflictK` revert `NotActive` at check (3): a record above `conflictK` is already void, and a confirmation in CONFLICT would only finalize a record faster that the time rule may still finalize. A call on a CONFLICTED record, record `conflictK` itself or an earlier conflicted index left above `conflictK` after a lowering, fails check (1) first and reverts `NotConflict`, on either path (revised 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 3, L1-R3-01 check-order follow-up; earlier: "The confirm path, and any conflict at `k′ ≥ conflictK`, reverts `NotActive`", which check (1)'s order contradicted for a CONFLICTED record). **[proven: a conflicted record never finalizes, from C2-R13's pass and this rule]** Every CONFLICTED index is at least `conflictK`, because `conflictK` only falls. In CONFLICT the pass stops strictly before `conflictK`, and landings, which would run the pass in normal mode, are refused. The restart clears the ring. So no CONFLICTED record becomes FINAL, and its end is never written as a checkpoint. In particular, a counterproof of an earlier record `j` that arrives after a conflict on `k > j` and before `j` is time-finalizable stops `j` from finalizing (E22 (i)). **Adversary schedule [assumed: disclosed exposure; added 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 3 Lows]:** once CONFLICT is entered, the holder of an unsound counterproving leaf can conflict every earlier PROVISIONAL record down to `first`, true ones included, each conflict lowering `conflictK`. Each such record then stops time-finalizing for as long as the DAO deliberates, which nothing bounds, and the restart's rollback grows from `conflictK`'s start to `first`'s: at most the records landed within DEGRADED_FINALITY before the call (an older one is finalized by the call's own pass, C2-R13), within the ring bound and ROLE_HORIZON. No false checkpoint is written, since the pass only stops earlier, and each extra conflict leaves the DAO another pair of leaf-accepted journals with equal fixed inputs. Safety is unaffected; the cost is a larger rollback (E22 (vii)). **[assumed: arbiter answer, D72]** Every entry point refused in CONFLICT, and every Etna entry point refused before activation except the forced-inclusion saves, which revert C4-R14's `ForceIntakeClosed` (D72 question 10) **[assumed: the specific answer governs]**, reverts `NotActive`: in CONFLICT, `land` and `landFor` of either mode, the hatch (C3-R07), `fiClear` (C3-R06a), `announceLanding` (C2-R08; added 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 4, L1-R4-02: an announcement keyed to the frozen pre-restart `lastLanded` would bond against a range the restart rewinds below), and `confirmProvisional`'s confirm path or a conflict above `conflictK`, each on a PROVISIONAL record that passes C2-R13's checks (1) and (2) (added 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 3, L1-R3-01; revised 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 3, L1-R3-01 check-order follow-up: a call on a CONFLICTED record reverts `NotConflict` from check (1), not `NotActive`). **[assumed: arbiter answer, D72]** D72 removed `ProofConflicted`, which had no trigger. The CONFLICT entry is an accepted write: it records the conflict and emits `ProofConflict`. Refused calls in CONFLICT revert `NotActive`, or `NotConflict` on a CONFLICTED record (C2-R13 check (1)). (Earlier: "D72 binds `ProofConflicted` to this CONFLICT entry; … [open: arbiter, C8-traceability §3.1 question 5]"; corrected 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 3 Lows, to match D72's removal.) (B's W12 verdict on #22196 (2026-10-02, comment 5945458948), item 1: the predicate at `6e2b732` let only `endBlockHash`, `endStateRoot` and `endPhHash` differ.)

**[proven: a conflict convicts one of the two leaves, conditional on C2-R04's partition claim]** `recorded` and `J′` are equal in every fixed input, the forced-inclusion fields included, and they differ. By C2-R04 a sound guest admits at most one journal for those inputs (C3-R06's claim), so at least one of the two leaves accepted a false journal. A holder of a sound leaf therefore cannot raise a conflict against a sound record. At `4aa0299` (c) was needed because a guest answer about due-ness entered derived outputs; under D41 no such answer exists.

**[proven: the honest counterproof is accepted, conditional on T3 for the counterproving leaf]** Let the sound guest accept the range with the record's fixed inputs, its journal `J*`, and let the record's journal differ from `J*` in any derived outputs: B's schedule, a faulty leaf proving false `endBlockHash`, `endStateRoot`, `endPhHash` and `endParentPhHash` for a two-block range whose false end a colluding quorum certifies (at `4aa0299` also a false `fiHeadAfter`, which under D41 is a fixed input and cannot differ). A false forced-inclusion claim is the other schedule, stated in C2-R04's landing-01 claim (2026-10-03): there the fixed inputs are the false ones, and the sound guest's journal differs from the record in `fiBlocksMatch`. A sound leaf proves `J*`, which passes (c) because its forced-inclusion fields are `recorded`'s; it equals the record's journal in every fixed input and differs in a derived output, so it is a conflict, and the record never finalizes. Vector E12 (§14). **Residual [assumed]:** a record over a range for which the sound guest admits no journal with the record's fixed inputs (a block that fails C2-R04 in the guest, certified by a colluding quorum; not a forced-inclusion claim that the range's blocks do not bear out, for which the guest always admits the `fiBlocksMatch = false` journal, C2-R04, landing-01, 2026-10-03) has no counterproof and finalizes after DEGRADED_FINALITY; that is the case the mode's argument below leaves to T11 (L-PF). The claim above is about acceptance, not availability: the counterprover also needs the guest's private witnesses, and all of them are public (the blobs, L1 headers, the queue entries' payloads, the parent state) except the carried certificate preimages of C2-R04's clause (i), which C2-R18 makes attesters retain only for RETAIN_SECONDS; a colluding quorum that formed a carried certificate only it holds can withhold that preimage and so prevent the counterproof. That quorum is the one the false root already needs (L5), so this is the same T11 case (L-PF), not a new premise.

**[assumed: the restart, field by field; C2's restored-field schema owed to C4-R12]** The upgrade's reinitializer restarts the chain from the restored checkpoint, the newest L1 checkpoint, which by C2-R13 is the start of the oldest unfinalized record `r`, the record at the cursor's `first` (a pass finalizing records below `conflictK` may have moved it). The pass stops before `conflictK`, so `first ≤ conflictK`. C2-R13's check (1) admitted the conflict only for `first ≤ k < next`, so `r` exists whenever CONFLICT does (added 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 3, L1-R3-01, L1-R3-02; earlier "(a pass finalizing records ahead of `k` may have moved it)", with no case for a conflict that leaves no unfinalized record). Its state is set as follows (C4-R12; A's verdict on C4 at `d1ebe1e`, #22205 comment 5945385606; C3-R05):

- **Restored from `r`, read from inbox storage, never from calldata.** `lastLanded.height`, `blockHash` and `stateRoot` (`r`'s start fields); the forced-inclusion head, `r.fiHeadAtStart` (C3-R10); `lastLanded.phHash`, `lastLanded.parentPhHash` and `lastVCHash`, from a preimage the upgrade supplies that must hash to `r.startDigest` (C2-R13). **Read at execution (added red-team cycle 2 round 5, RST-01, 2026-10-04) [assumed: proposed rule; open: C4-R12 adoption by B]:** the reinitializer reads `first`, `conflictK` and `r` from live storage when it executes, not from values fixed when the DAO prepared the payload, and validates the supplied preimage against that live `r.startDigest`; a payload that pins a record index or a `conflictK` reverts as a whole if either changed while it waited (a `finalizeProvisional` pass, or a `confirmProvisional` call's pass, can move `first` up to `conflictK`, and a lowering conflict can lower `conflictK`), so the restart never restores from a stale preimage. **[proven: from C2-R13's pass and this rule's lowering predicate]** once `first == conflictK` neither can change: the pass stops before `conflictK`, and a lowering needs a PROVISIONAL record `first ≤ k′ < conflictK`, of which there is none; so a payload prepared at `first == conflictK` (after a pass has finalized every aged record below it) cannot be invalidated by a later call, and the DAO prepares it there **[assumed: procedure]**. (Earlier the rule named only "a preimage the upgrade supplies", silent on which record it pins, so a permissionless pass or a lowering conflict between preparation and execution made the payload revert; the floor half of the finding, C4-R12's D74 check, reads live storage and was refuted.) The restart regime's opening object, its domain-separated encoding and how the first block after the restart names it are owed by S2 and C8 (C4-R12), **open**.
- **Initialized at the restart block.** `restartL1Number = block.number` and `restartTimestamp = block.timestamp` (C4-R12); `lastLanded.anchorTipNumber = restartL1Number` and `lastLanded.anchorTipTimestamp = restartTimestamp`, the anchor and `l1Ref` floor; `lastLanded.at = restartTimestamp`; `lastLandedTerm = firstPermittedTerm − 1`, with `firstPermittedTerm` C4-R07's formula evaluated at `restartTimestamp`; the queue's `base = restartTimestamp + HATCH_DELAY − HATCH_GRACE` and `short` cleared, `hatchAt` and `hatchL1` untouched (C3-R10); C2-R08's deferral flag unspent; the provisional ring, its cursor and the CONFLICT flag cleared. `lastLanded.termId` and `view` are not restored; their values belong to the restart regime S2 and C8 owe (**open**).
- **Voided at the restart (added 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 4, L1-R4-02) [assumed: proposed rule]:** every outstanding landing announcement (C2-R08) is void and its ANNOUNCE_BOND is returned to its announcer's ledger, with no forfeiture, so no post-restart landing, dead-mode style or not, settles a pre-restart announcement. The reinitializer refunds them, or, if the announcement record's layout (unplaced, C8-R15) makes enumeration unbounded, marks them void so that each settles by refund on its announcer's call **[open: C2/C8, the record's placement]**. This is the restart's one ledger change. Earlier the restart named no announcement, and its bond, a ledger amount "neither restored nor reset", could be read as forfeited by the first post-restart landing covering its `firstHeight`, a dead-mode-style landing and so a replacement (C2-R09); vector E23 (v).
- **Neither restored nor reset.** C3's per-entry status bits, `legacySettled` and fee words (D18 kept: settlement is irreversible, C3-R10); term records, reserves and ledgers (except the announcement refunds above); recorded view changes and pinned assignments. The re-consumption of `[r.fiHeadAtStart, headBefore)` writes only the replayable bits (`legacyReplayable` for a legacy id): set on a re-void, cleared on a re-execution (C3-R03, C3-R10).

Under C3 at `4aa0299` the restart had to set `fiRun` and `fiClearTip`, and a clear tip lowered by the first block after the restart could void and burn an entry saved during the DAO's CONFLICT halt (B's W12 verdict on C3 at `4aa0299`, #22200 comment 5945484944, its additional Medium). D41 withdraws both words: the restart sets `base` from `restartTimestamp`, so no window opens before `restartTimestamp + HATCH_DELAY` and no derived clock can regress at the restart edge (C3-R10's proven claim), which closes that item on C3's side (§16, C3 row). That every anchor and every present `l1Ref` of the restarted chain, not only a range's tip, is at or above `restartL1Number` is C4-R12's requirement; L7 bounds the tip only, so the per-block floor is C7's V5 genesis-floor rule applied to the restart, **open** on C7 and C4 (§16). The earlier text set `lastLanded` "as `initEtna` does" and keyed `fiClearTip` on C4's `drainedAt`; `initEtna` sets no `lastLanded`, and C4-R07 now initializes from the activation block (`fiClearTip = activationTimestamp`, D25), so the list above replaces both (B's W12 verdict on #22196, follow-up items).

The chain restarts dead-mode style (S1): the blocks built on the voided landing and the two-leaf landings between the restored checkpoint and the conflicting record are replaced, and the entries those landings consumed, `[r.fiHeadAtStart, headBefore)` with `headBefore` the head before the upgrade, are re-consumed in order under C3-R06, each settled id moving no money (C3-R10). Fees credited for those entries, rewards paid and term records settled by the voided landings are not clawed back, a bounded leak (L-PF). The re-consumption is funded and idempotent by C3's per-entry irreversible settlement (C3-R03, C3-R10 at `76c6150`; D18 kept): a re-consumed entry is re-executed or re-voided and never credited, refunded or burned again, so no second refund needs funding; C4-R12 states the same for this rewind (C4 `d1ebe1e`), closed when C3 and C4 merge (B's W8 verdict on #22196 (2026-10-01), cross-section obligation). The upgrade's effect on the L1 checkpoint map is C2-R21's obligation on C1, C4 and C8.

**[assumed: limitation of the CONFLICT rollback, after B's P-B-C1-03 (#22195 at 75616d5), item (b)]** Hiding checkpoint records above a floor (C2-R21) changes what the SignalService returns; it does not revoke the received-signal cache bits the Bridge wrote when a message was proven against a record, nor undo Bridge effects already executed on L1 against such a record (a withdrawal paid out, a message marked done or retriable). So L1-side effects consumed against a record of the rolled-back history are not undone by the rollback. On C2's path as written this has no trigger: no record of the rolled-back history is ever readable on L1, because every height above the restored checkpoint was never written (the restart claim below), so no message can have been proven against one. The limitation binds the two cases outside that claim, and the exposure differs by case. Effects are bounded by the Bridge quota, a rate the existing Bridge enforces (C6's L-OP: the quota bounds the loss rate), times the time the record was readable. (i) A bug in C2-R13 or C2-R15 that writes a record above an unfinalized provisional record k: that record can be contested only within DEGRADED_FINALITY of k (C2-R13), so the readable time before the conflict is recorded is at most one DEGRADED_FINALITY window, 86,400 s, and the exposure, derived, is one window of withdrawals at the quota, plus the quota times the DAO's latency from `ProofConflict` to the upgrade, which nothing here bounds (unmeasured, a governance figure). (ii) A rollback upgrade that the DAO extends below a published root (which C2-R21 forbids on its own; a DAO may still choose it): the rolled-back checkpoints were written by two-leaf landings or finalization passes, possibly long before record k, so their readable time has no DEGRADED_FINALITY bound; the exposure is the quota times the time since the oldest rolled-back published checkpoint was written, a figure the DAO's choice sets and C2 does not bound (**open**: C2 relies on C2-R21's prohibition, and C4's rollback upgrade must state the figure if it departs from it). The quota's value is the Bridge's and is not a C2 number. C4's rollback upgrade table must state which caches the reinitializer clears and which effects stay; C6 carries this on the accepted-limitations register beside L-PF (§16). (Landing §7, §10; interfaces §1; after the #22188 review and its fix pass; the restart claim below after J's review of #22196 (2026-10-01), J-1.)

**[proven: the restart writes no height already holding a record, by C2-R13 and C2-R15]** The restored checkpoint is the newest one written, the start of the oldest unfinalized record (C2-R13). Above it lie only one-leaf landings, which write no checkpoint (C2-R13), and two-leaf landings covered by that unfinalized record, which write none (C2-R15); so every height the restarted chain re-lands was never written, each re-landing write meets an empty height, and C1-R06's conflict case (C1-R07 on L1) is never reached. This is the property the restart relies on. J's review of #22196 (2026-10-01) named the collision it avoids: "C1-R06 is first-write-wins ... C4's rollback re-lands L2 blocks from an earlier finalized point, producing different block hashes at the same heights ... those writes revert and the rollback cannot checkpoint progress past the first previously written height"; the restart is conflict-free only because the rolled-back heights hold no record, and C2-R21 binds the rollback upgrade so that this does not depend on C2-R13 and C2-R15 staying as written.

**[proven: rollback bound]** The rollback stops at the parent of the conflicting record (the record at `conflictK`, the lowest conflicted one; 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 3, L1-R3-01) once every earlier record is final, otherwise at the start of the oldest unfinalized record: by the ring bound at most one DEGRADED_FINALITY of landings, whose terms lie inside ROLE_HORIZON (§12), so the restart needs no role of an older term.

**[proven: what the mode buys, conditional on T11]** A false root in single-proof mode needs one ZK bug, a colluding quorum (L5 ties the journal's end hash to a certified header whose state root Q attesters executed, V6) and no honest proof of the other leaf over a journal with the record's fixed inputs (C2-R04's partition) for DEGRADED_FINALITY. When that leaf is unusable for so long, the premise of the mode, the window adds delay, not evidence, and safety rests on T11: elapsed time is why the landings are provisional, not why they are safe. This is the one place attester execution carries part of state safety, and only after DEGRADE_AFTER of stalled landing.

**[proven: from C2-R04's `fiBlocksMatch` claim, §14 E12 (vi) and C3-R06 step 5; scoped 2026-10-03, B-W22-A02] The quorum premise covers false execution-root publication only.** The paragraph above concerns a false root, which L5 ties to a certified header and so needs a colluding quorum. A false forced-inclusion disposition needs no quorum. In single-proof mode, one faulty leaf can land a truly executed, honestly certified range that carries no request block with a false `fiHeadAfter`, `fiVoidCount` or `fiReplayIds` (E12 (vi)): L5 accepts the true end PH, and C3-R06 step 5 settles the falsely claimed entries at landing (status bits, the includer's bounty credit or the void burn). That record finalizes, disposition included, unless a sound counterproof of the `fiBlocksMatch = false` journal arrives within DEGRADED_FINALITY (C2-R04's contestability claim), so its finality needs the absence of a timely sound counterproof, not a quorum. Even after a timely conflict, the money moved at landing is not restored: the C3-R10 re-consumption moves no money for a settled id (C3-R06's claims; this is the same no-clawback mechanism as L-PF's bounded leak, cited for the mechanism only, not as coverage by L-PF's signed scope, which stays open below; earlier "(C3-R06's claims, L-PF's bounded leak)", qualified 2026-10-03 on B's recheck of `e6b795c`). Gain for that lander: a false bounty or a false void of a requester's deposit, plus recovery disruption through CONFLICT; cost: one faulty proof and one landing. **[signed: D79 (4)]** L-PF's signed scope (D73 (6)) covers this one-faulty-leaf forced-inclusion settlement, the unrestored landing-time money included (2026-10-04; earlier "[open: user sign-off] whether L-PF's signed scope (D73 (6)) covers this one-faulty-leaf forced-inclusion settlement; this page does not assume so") (relabelled 2026-10-03 on A's recheck of the revision, earlier "[open: user decision]"). `fiBlocksMatch` makes it contestable; it does not make the landing-time settlement reversible.

### C2-R15. The L1 checkpoint write

**[assumed: proposed rule]** From the upgraded Inbox proxy (the immutable `_authorizedSyncer` of the L1 SignalService, address unchanged), the inbox calls

```solidity
_signalService.saveCheckpoint(ICheckpointStore.Checkpoint({
    blockNumber: uint48(endHeight), blockHash: endBlockHash, stateRoot: endStateRoot }));
```

exactly: once per landing accepted with ZK_K leaves unless an unfinalized provisional record precedes it on its chain (then none: the landing stores no record, needs no confirmation of its own and is subsumed by the checkpoint written when the newest record it follows is finalized); and once per finalization pass of C2-R13, with the tuple that pass selects. In transaction order the call follows L11's `verify`, a view call, and is made only when the returned `zkLeaves_` does not make the landing provisional (C2-R05 L11, C2-R12); any later revert rolls it back (revised 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 2, L1-01; earlier "In transaction order the call precedes `verifyProof` and is rolled back by its revert", which fixed the write before the accepting leaves were known). `ICheckpointStore` is unchanged, and so is the SignalService's storage until a C2-R21 rollback, which adds only the epoch floor list in the reserved gap and keeps every epoch-1 slot; the inbox enforces strictly increasing heights, which the SignalService does not check (it already rejects zero hashes with `SS_INVALID_CHECKPOINT`, `SignalService.sol:176-177`; the Anchor page's "checks none" is a misstatement, §16 item 9). C1 owns the L1 write semantics: C2's writes obey C1-R06 as C1-R07 applies it on L1 (a different tuple at an existing height reverts with `EtnaCheckpointConflict`, an exact duplicate is a silent no-op), and C2 does not weaken either case. A landing never triggers either case: the inbox's L1 sequence check (`firstHeight == lastLanded.height + 1` with `endHeight >= firstHeight`, a range holding at least one block, C2-R05) makes every landing's `endHeight` exceed `lastLanded.height`, so end heights are strictly increasing, and no height is written twice (proven below). On the landing path the rule is therefore defensive, reachable only through a bug or through an L1 reorg, which reverts inbox state together with the write (C2-R20), so a re-landing after a reorg meets an empty height again. Under EIP-8037 the two fresh words cost about 196k state gas per write, paid by the lander, unavoidable under R2. (Landing §8; Anchor page §3; after the #22188 review; the deferral to C1-R06 after J's review of #22196 (2026-10-01), J-1, which found that "a normative rule cannot both govern the write and be disclaimed by the section that produces the writes".) **Adversary schedule:** publication of a retractable root (never before confirmation or the window; after it, C2-R14's residual); Shasta prove and Etna land writing in one interval (C4 proves none exists); a write at an occupied height (unreachable from a landing or a pass, below; a rollback is C2-R14 and C2-R21).

**[proven: no height is written twice, conditional on C2-R13's in-order finalization]** C2 writes heights of two kinds. A two-leaf landing with no unfinalized record ahead writes its `endHeight`, above `lastLanded.height` by L1 and so above every earlier write, each of which lies at or below `lastLanded`. A finalization pass that finalizes at least one record writes the start of the oldest record left unfinalized, or `lastLanded` when none is left; a pass that finalizes nothing writes nothing (C2-R13). A record's start is the end of the landing before it: if an unfinalized record preceded that landing it wrote nothing (this rule), and otherwise it wrote its end, in which case no record older than the one in question is unfinalized at any later pass, so no later pass selects that start. `lastLanded` is selected only after a record is finalized, so it has advanced past every earlier write of it. Every write therefore lands on an empty height, and C1-R06's conflict and duplicate cases are unreachable from C2's writes.

### C2-R21. Rollback floors in the checkpoint map

**[assumed: proposed rule, placed as an obligation on C1 (owner of C1-R06), C4 (owner of the rollback upgrade) and C8 (owner of the layout)]** A rollback (C4's forward upgrade after CONFLICT, C2-R14) must leave no record of the rolled-back history readable above the restored checkpoint and must let the re-landing write at those heights, without overwrite semantics and without resetting the map. Shape: the SignalService's checkpoint mapping `mapping(uint256 => mapping(uint48 => CheckpointRecord))` at slot 254, whose outer key is today the constant `VERSION = 1`, becomes epoch-keyed, and the rollback upgrade appends one floor `(epoch + 1, floorHeight)`, `floorHeight` the restored checkpoint's height, to the floor list C1-R10 places at slot 255 of the reserved gap (its element layout C8's). The epoch a read or a write at a height uses is selected by C1-R06's lookup, which C2 cites and does not restate (append order decides; equality with a floor does not select it; epoch 1 when no floor matches; a read whose selected epoch holds no record is not-found and never falls back), and C2 appends only as C1-R10 admits: once per CONFLICT restart, through the reinitializer, at the newest written checkpoint height. **[proven: C2's floors are nondecreasing, by C2-R05, C2-R14 and C2-R15]** A floor is the restored checkpoint, the newest checkpoint written (C2-R14); every later write lies above it, since after the restart `lastLanded` is the floor and the next landing starts at `lastLanded + 1` (C2-R05) and end heights strictly increase (C2-R15); so the next floor, the newest checkpoint written at that later restart, is at or above it, equal when nothing was written in between. This is the nondecreasing rule C1-R06 states for C2's current recoveries, and C4-R12 rejects a lower floor; C1's V12 vector of a decreasing floor tests the lookup for a hypothetical later upgrade and is no C2 path. **[proven: the restored record stays readable and every write meets the newest epoch, by C1-R06's equality rule]** The checkpoint at a floor height `F` was written under the epoch in force before that rollback; a read at `F` does not select the floor at `F`, so it returns that record; a write at `F` is unreachable (the next landing starts at `F + 1`), and every write lies above the newest floor, where C1-R06 selects the newest epoch for reads and writes alike. So no record of the rolled-back history is reachable above the floor, nothing is overwritten, and storage stays compatible (C1-R10: epoch-1 records keep their slots and one gap slot is the only new storage); the floor cases are vector E06 (§14). Under C2-R14's restart claim no record exists above the restored checkpoint on the C2 path as written, so the rule has no reachable trigger today; it binds the upgrade so that the restart's correctness rests on the map's shape rather than on C2-R13 and C2-R15 staying unchanged. (J's review of #22196 (2026-10-01), J-1; the arbiter's decision; the read rule after B's P-B-C1-03 (#22195 at 75616d5), item (a), now owned by C1-R06; the earlier "floors may decrease" and the duplicated lookup dropped under D28 and B's W12 verdict on #22196 (2026-10-02, comment 5945458948), follow-up items; rejected alternatives in §15.) **Adversary schedule:** a reader asks for a rolled-back height (the epoch in force returns the re-landed record or none, never the voided one); a rollback upgrade that overwrites, resets, exempts heights or appends a lower floor (§15, C4-R12); a read made costly by many rollbacks (the scan grows by one per DAO event, not per write).

## 7. Landing rewards and the reserve seam

### C2-R16. The reward formulas

**[assumed: proposed formulas; every TAIKO amount is a formula on inputs S3 marks unmeasured, limitation L15]** The lander is paid in TAIKO on the existing L1 bond ledger from the landing reserve of the sequencer whose blocks it lands (C2-R17). For a landing at L1 time `τ`:

```text
landableFrom(t) = max(termEnd(t), lastLanded.at)      fixed in t's term record by the first landing that reaches t
ramp(x, τ, t)   = x_min + (x_max − x_min) · clamp((τ − landableFrom(t)) / LAND_WINDOW, 0, 1)
r(τ, t)         = ramp over [R_BLK_MIN, R_BLK_MAX]      per block
r_land(τ, t)    = ramp over [0, R_LAND_MAX]              per paid landing
PIN_REWARD(τ,t) = ramp over [0, PIN_MAX]                 to the first pinner of t, when that pin is made by a landing that settles t
PIN_REWARD_RA(τ,t) = PIN_MAX · clamp((τ − landableFrom(t) − LAND_WINDOW) / LAND_WINDOW, 0, 1)
                                                         to the first pinner of t, when that pin is a recordAssignment(t) (revision 2026-10-04, ECON-PIN-SNIPE)
reward          = Σ_segments blocks · r(τ, t) + R_BLOB · min(blobs, 15 − blobsPaid(t))
                  + r_land(τ, t) · [k ≤ ⌈bytesLanded_after(t) / LANDING_UNIT⌉]
attester share  = ATT_REWARD_PER_BLOCK per landed block, debited from each block's holder, split equally among the end certificate's signers other than seats owned by the landed term's holder, the excluded part staying in the reserve (decision D15, adopted from the slashing page §6; S3-R16 cites this sentence)
```

`k` counts the landings whose first segment belongs to `t`; `bytesLanded` is the term's sequencer-block records landed so far (what V10 bounds); `blobsPaid` counts blobs already paid for the term; FI records earn nothing. A sequencer that knows its prover is down may shortcut the block ramp with a signed `RewardBoost{termId, minRewardPerBlockGwei, seqSig}`, capped at R_BLK_MAX. For a self-landing (the proof's bound `rewardTo`, C2-R02, resolves to the holder, whoever submits the transaction) the lander's debit and credit are both skipped. `lastLanded.at` in `landableFrom` is the landing the first range reaching `t` builds on, so every landing of a term ramps from one origin and no term is charged for time in which nothing could land; a `recordAssignment(t)` pin is paid only for a term at or below the first unlanded term and at or above `firstEtnaTerm` (C4-R07); a pin of a term below `firstEtnaTerm` is accepted as S1-R14 states, pays no PIN_REWARD and debits no reserve (revision 2026-10-03, migration-bridge-01; earlier: "paid only for a term at or below the first unlanded term"). (Landing §5; round 3, H2; round 4, R4H-7; round 5, R5H-2 and fix pass; round 6, R6H-2, R6H-3 and fix pass; round 2, S6.)

**[assumed: proposed rule; the grace length LAND_WINDOW and the grace itself proposed, pending user sign-off] (revision 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 3, ECON-PIN-SNIPE)** A pin of `t` made by `recordAssignment(t)` is paid `PIN_REWARD_RA`, whose ramp starts LAND_WINDOW after `landableFrom(t)`; for such a pin `landableFrom(t)` is `max(termEnd(t), lastLanded.at)` read at the pin, `t` being the first unlanded term in the only payable case. A pin made by a landing that settles `t` is paid `PIN_REWARD` from `landableFrom(t)` as before. Either is skipped when the pinner is the holder (S1-R14), and only the first pin of `t` is paid. **[proven: a holder that self-lands `t` before `landableFrom(t) + LAND_WINDOW` pays no PIN_REWARD for `t`, conditional on the holder's landing being the first landing to reach `t`, from the two formulas and S1-R14's holder skip]** The first pin of `t` is then either a `recordAssignment(t)` made before the holder's landing, so at `τ < landableFrom(t) + LAND_WINDOW`, where `PIN_REWARD_RA = 0`, or the holder's own landing, whose pin is skipped. (Revised 2026-10-04, red-team cycle 2 round 3 Lows; earlier the claim was unconditional and closed with "no other landing settled `t` first, or the holder's landing would not land `t`", which overlooks a landing that settles only part of `t`.) **Partial-landing front-run [open: disclosed residual, beside the late self-lander below]:** landings may split a term (sixty one-block landings, below), and a landing pins every term it settles, so a third party can land only `t`'s first block, with its own proof, before the holder's landing. Its pin is paid PIN_REWARD from `landableFrom(t)` and its landing r_land (`k = 1` is always paid), at `+ 140` about 31.11 + 101.11 TAIKO plus its block's `r` and blobs' R_BLOB, all from the holder's reserve; the holder's later landing of the rest of `t` is still a self-landing. The third party needs a one-block proof within about 200 s and a landing of about 2 M gas, so this is a proving race, marginally profitable at low fees, not a free snipe. Extending the grace to landing pins whose `rewardTo` is not the holder would remove the pin part, leave r_land and the 7,161 exposure unchanged; it is not adopted here. Vector: E07's partial-landing case. Before this revision a third party's `recordAssignment(t)` at a lag of 140 to 150 s took `400 × 150 / 1,800 = 33.33` TAIKO from such a holder, about half of V_term per self-landed term, about 2,000 TAIKO per hour chain-wide, enough to take an untopped 8-seat owner at the launch registry past its lapse line in about 11 days; its only defence was a pre-pin of every own term at a full walk's gas (ECON-PIN-SNIPE). **[assumed: what the grace keeps and costs]** The per-term exposure is unchanged: one pin, at most PIN_MAX. A `recordAssignment` pin earns nothing in the first LAND_WINDOW, and C2 does not need one then: a term that lands is pinned by its landing, at a gas cost the landing reward pays (S1-R14's congestion case), and a term not pinned inside ROLE_HORIZON is unlandable whatever happens to it (C2-R10). **[open: disclosed residual, a choice for the user with S3]** A holder that self-lands more than LAND_WINDOW after `landableFrom(t)` (a slow prover, or waiting out a fee above break-even) can still lose a third party's pin reward, up to PIN_MAX at `landableFrom(t) + 2 × LAND_WINDOW`, unless it pins `t` itself first at a full walk's gas (about 0.8 M at the launch registry, S1 §11). The alternatives not adopted are a deferred credit (the pinner is recorded and paid only when `t` settles by a landing whose `rewardTo` is not the holder, one more term-record field for C8) and no paid `recordAssignment` pin at all. Vector: E07's snipe case.

**Failure behaviour [assumed: stated from the rule text, no behaviour added]:** no failure path of its own; the formulas are definitional. They are evaluated among C2-R06's effects, after L1 to L9 and before L11's verification, and rolled back with the whole landing if L11 fails, so a landing that reverts pays nothing (C2-R06's failure statement), and the rule states no condition under which a landing is refused for its reward. Where a term falls outside a payment condition (`k` above the `⌈bytesLanded_after(t) / LANDING_UNIT⌉` bound, `blobsPaid(t)` at 15, an FI record) the stated outcome is a zero term of the sum, not a refusal. For a pin above the first unlanded term, or of a term below `firstEtnaTerm`, C2-R16 pays no PIN_REWARD for it and adds no refusal; whether `recordAssignment` refuses the pin is S1-R14's (it does not, for either case; the second case added 2026-10-03, migration-bridge-01). **[open: S3]** What happens when the debited reserve holds less than the computed amount is not stated here: C2-R17 relies on invariant I2 as S3 states it. Vectors: E07, E10 and E14.

**[proven: no reward-type movement debits a reserve for a term below `firstEtnaTerm`, conditional on C4-R07's initialization and on L4 admitting only terms above `lastLandedTerm`] (revision 2026-10-03, migration-bridge-01)** C4-R07 initializes `lastLandedTerm = firstEtnaTerm − 1`, and a CONFLICT restart sets it to `firstPermittedTerm − 1`, which is at least `firstEtnaTerm − 1` because C4-R07's formula is nondecreasing in its timestamp and `restartTimestamp > activationTimestamp`; `lastLandedTerm` never decreases otherwise, so no landing reaches a term below `firstEtnaTerm` and the block ramp, R_BLOB, `r_land`, a `RewardBoost` and the attester share, each per landed block or landing of the term, are never computed for one. The only other movement, PIN_REWARD, is zero below `firstEtnaTerm` by the floor above. Before this revision a pin made after activation of any unpinned term `t < firstEtnaTerm` with `termStart(t) ≥ now − ROLE_HORIZON` passed the only stated condition (at or below the first unlanded term, which is `firstEtnaTerm`), was no longer refused `NotActive` (C2-R14 refuses `recordAssignment` only before activation; its CONFLICT refusals name `land`, `landFor`, the hatch and `fiClear`, not the pin), and ramped to PIN_MAX from `landableFrom(t)`, so every pre-activation term in the horizon could debit up to 400 TAIKO from a reserve deposited before activation.
